For $d=(d_{p,r})\in\overline D_B$, regard $d_{p,r}$ as a vector
of $Q_{p,r}H$. Then
\[
 \overline{\delta B}
 =\bigcap_{r\in\Q^\times}\bigcap_{p\ne q}
   \ker\bigl(d\mapsto Q_{q,r}d_{p,r}-Q_{p,r}d_{q,r}\bigr).
 \tag{MQ19}\label{mq:eq:compatibility}
\]
The unlabelled assertion is obtained by omitting $r$ and using
$Q_p$. Every displayed map is bounded with norm at most $\sqrt2$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Coordinate extraction and each $Q$ are contractions. The triangle
and Cauchy--Schwarz inequalities give the stated bound. Commutation
of the $Q$ projections proves that these maps kill every generic
diagonal and hence its closure. Conversely, decompose into the
orthogonal channels. In channel $a=(r,T)$, its $p$ coordinate is
zero outside $J_a$. For $p,q\in J_a$ the displayed equation reads
$d_{a,p}=d_{a,q}$, because both projections act as the identity.
Thus every channel is diagonal. Theorem~\ref{mq:thm:closure} gives
exactly \eqref{mq:eq:compatibility}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The generic diagonal as an actual closed unbounded operator}

The preceding positivity does not require the generic diagonal to
be a bounded operator in the original generic $L^2$ norm. In fact
it is unbounded. The precise receiving operator is as follows.

\begin{theorem}[Domain, adjoint, and closed range]
\label{mq:thm:closed-diagonal}
Let $\mathscr H_W=\widehat\bigoplus_aH_a$ denote $H$ or $H_B$.
The closure of the algebraic operator $\delta:W\to\overline D_W$
is
\[
 \Dom\delta_{\max}=
 \left\{h=(h_a):\sum_a k_a\|h_a\|^2<\infty\right\},
 \qquad(\delta_{\max}h)_{a,p}=h_a\quad(p\in J_a).
 \tag{MQ20}\label{mq:eq:closed-domain}
\]
Here $h$ is also required to belong to $\mathscr H_W$, so the
unmarked $k_a=0$ part has its usual square summability. Its range
is the closed subspace \eqref{mq:eq:diagonal-closure}. Its adjoint is
\[
 (\delta_{\max}^*d)_a=\sum_{p\in J_a}d_{a,p},\qquad
 \Dom\delta_{\max}^*=
 \left\{d:\sum_a\left\|\sum_{p\in J_a}d_{a,p}\right\|^2
                         <\infty\right\}.
 \tag{MQ21}\label{mq:eq:closed-adjoint}
\]
On an unmarked channel the adjoint value is zero. The positive
operator $\delta_{\max}^*\delta_{\max}$ is the multiplication
operator $N:h_a\mapsto k_ah_a$, with domain
$\sum_a k_a^2\|h_a\|^2<\infty$ in $\mathscr H_W$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The squared norm of an algebraic diagonal is exactly
$\sum_a k_a\|h_a\|^2$. Convergence of a vector and its diagonal
implies convergence in each channel; the limiting marked
coordinates are therefore the same coefficient in each mark.
This proves closedness of the operator in
\eqref{mq:eq:closed-domain}. Conversely truncate a vector to finitely
many channels and approximate each remaining $h_a$ by its dense
algebraic tests. The graph norm on that channel is
$(1+k_a)^{1/2}\|h_a\|$, so these approximations converge in
graph norm. Thus this closed operator is exactly the closure of
the original operator, not an unrelated extension.

A vector in \eqref{mq:eq:diagonal-closure} has coefficients $z_a$
repeated $k_a$ times with $\sum k_a\|z_a\|^2<\infty$. Setting
the unmarked coefficients to zero makes $(z_a)$ a vector of
$\mathscr H_W$, since every nonzero $k_a$ is at least one.
It belongs to the displayed domain and maps to that vector.
This proves the closed-range assertion. Pairing against arbitrary
finite-channel tests shows that the adjoint is the marked sum;
this functional is bounded on $\mathscr H_W$ exactly under the
square-summability condition \eqref{mq:eq:closed-adjoint}. Applying
it to a diagonal multiplies by $k_a$. The domains then give
precisely the asserted domain of $N$.
\end{proof}

The restriction to the orthogonal complement of its kernel has a
bounded inverse on the range. There is also a bounded generalized
inverse on all of $\overline D_W$: send a marked vector to
$\bar d_a$ for $k_a>0$ and to zero for $k_a=0$. Indeed
\[
 \sum_a k_a\|\bar d_a\|^2\le\sum_{a,p}\|d_{a,p}\|^2.
 \tag{MQ22}\label{mq:eq:mean-bound}
\]
Thus this mean lies in the actual closed domain and its diagonal
is the orthogonal projection onto \eqref{mq:eq:diagonal-closure}.

For a concrete failure of boundedness, choose finite sets $T_n$
with $|T_n|=n$, and a test $h_n\in V_{T_n}^\perp$ with
$\|h_n\|=1$. Such tests exist: at $p$ the nonzero test
$1_{p\Z_p}-p^{-1}\xi_p$ is orthogonal to $\xi_p$, and its
normalization, together with a normalized real Schwartz function,
gives the required tensor. Then $\|\delta h_n\|=\sqrt n$.
Also $\delta W$ itself need not be closed: choose distinct primes
$p_n$ and unit tests in the corresponding singleton channels.
The convergent marked sum $\sum_n2^{-n}\delta h_n$ lies in
\eqref{mq:eq:diagonal-closure}, but cannot lie in $\delta W$ since
it has infinitely many nonzero channels. These facts verify the
need for the closure used in (CCF30)--(CCF32).

\subsection{The relative completion and the positive arithmetic image}

The algebraic splitting in \eqref{mq:eq:relative-exact} gives the
positive relative receiver chosen in (CCF27): give its $D_W$ the
marked norm and its remaining support groups chosen positive
finite-dimensional forms tensored with the generic $L^2$ norm.
All these forms are strictly positive on the original algebraic
groups. This is a valid specified receiver. It is useful to compute
its exact relation to completing the terms of
\eqref{mq:eq:relative-complex} in their original coefficient norms.

Give support cochains the norm that sums squared coefficient norms
over strict chains. Put $M=|S|>0$. On closed degree-one support
cochains, $c_s$ is constant on each connected component. Write
\[
 \bar c=M^{-1}\sum_{s\in S}c_s,\qquad
 c'_s=c_s-\bar c,\qquad \sum_sc'_s=0.
 \tag{MQ23}\label{mq:eq:support-mean}
\]
The number of points in each support component is thus retained
in the sum; it is not replaced by a component count. The closed
degree-zero boundary is the graph
$w\mapsto(\delta_{\max}w,\Delta w)$ on
$\Dom\delta_{\max}$, and it has closed range because its
second coordinate has norm $\sqrt M\|w\|$.

On an algebraic closed representative define
\[
 q=d-\delta\bar c,\qquad c'=c-\Delta\bar c.
 \tag{MQ24}\label{mq:eq:relative-coordinates}
\]
These are invariant under its actual boundaries. In a channel
with $k_a>0$, write $q_a=q_a^{\circ}+\Delta\bar q_a$, where
$\sum_{p\in J_a}q_{a,p}^{\circ}=0$. Its exact quotient norm
in this closed-graph Hilbert complex is
\[
 \|q_a^{\circ}\|^2+
       \frac{k_aM}{k_a+M}\|\bar q_a\|^2+
       \sum_{s\in S}\|c'_{a,s}\|^2.
 \tag{MQ25}\label{mq:eq:graph-relative-norm}
\]
For $k_a=0$ only the last summand remains. To prove the formula,
all representatives in that channel have the form
$(q_a+\Delta h,\Delta h+c'_a)$. Orthogonality of the two
mean-zero parts leaves the minimization
\[
 \inf_h\left\{\sum_{p\in J_a}\|q_{a,p}+h\|^2
                              +M\|h\|^2\right\}.
\]
Completing the square gives the minimizing value
$h=-(k_a+M)^{-1}\sum_pq_{a,p}$ and the value
$\|q_a\|^2-\|\sum_pq_{a,p}\|^2/(k_a+M)$, which is exactly
\eqref{mq:eq:graph-relative-norm}. Finite algebraic closed
representatives are dense in the full closed degree-one space:
truncate its channels and approximate the coefficient of each
support component by algebraic tests, retaining equality within
that component. Thus taking the orthogonal sum over all channels
proves the formula for the completed receiver. The coordinate $q$
after this completion belongs to the weighted space specified by
\eqref{mq:eq:graph-relative-norm}; its diagonal part need not belong
to the original unweighted $\overline D_W$. This is why no
unjustified application of $\delta_{\max}$ to an arbitrary
completed generic coefficient is required.

The diagonal $q$ coefficient has norm factor $k_aM/(k_a+M)$
in this receiver. On the subspace $c'=0$, the algebraically
split $D_W$ coordinate for every point choice is exactly $q$,
and its norm has factor $k_a$. Since $k_a$ is unbounded, the identity on the
algebraic relative groups does not identify these two completions
by a bounded inverse. This is an exact distinction between two
specified relative norms, not a failure of the arithmetic quotient.
Indeed the relative-to-arithmetic map on either receiver is
\[
 (q,c')\longmapsto q^{\circ}\in\mathscr M_W.
 \tag{MQ26}\label{mq:eq:completed-arithmetic-image}
\]
It is bounded and onto in the closed-graph receiver, by
\eqref{mq:eq:graph-relative-norm}; set $q=q^{\circ}$ and $c'=0$
for an isometric right inverse. On algebraic classes it is exactly
\eqref{mq:eq:arithmetic-map}, because $\delta\bar c$ is an actual
diagonal. Hence both completions retain exactly the same attained
positive mixed-place arithmetic quotient, with all labels and
marked multiplicities. The additional support components and
all higher support cycles remain present in their respective
relative groups.

\subsection{Scaling, Fourier, and the nonunit-prime defect}

The real action $\vartheta_\lambda f(x_\infty,x_f)
=f(\lambda^{-1}x_\infty,x_f)$ commutes with every $P_p$ and
has squared-norm factor $\lambda$. It preserves every orthogonal
channel and its marked set. It therefore gives on the actual
arithmetic quotient
\[
 \langle\vartheta_\lambda[d],\vartheta_\lambda[d']\rangle
       =\lambda\langle[d],[d']\rangle.
 \tag{MQ27}\label{mq:eq:scaling}
\]
This holds for all rational labels as well. It also preserves the
closed diagonal domain, since the weights $k_a$ are unaffected.
The finite-place Fourier transform fixes $\xi_p$ under the
specified conductor-$\Z_p$ characters and Haar measures. Its
unitarity therefore makes it commute with $P_p$: both the line
$\C\xi_p$ and its orthogonal complement are invariant. Full
Fourier is consequently unitary on each marked quotient and
preserves the generic diagonal and its closure. In the crossed
product the map additionally sends $r$ to $r^{-1}$; the equality
$D(r^{-1})=D(r)$ preserves every marked set. These observations
prove that the positive arithmetic receiver in
\eqref{mq:eq:positive-quotient} carries the claimed Fourier and
real-scaling maps. Their exact relation is
$\mathcal F\vartheta_\lambda
=\lambda\vartheta_{\lambda^{-1}}\mathcal F$, obtained by the
real change of variable $x_\infty=\lambda t$.

For comparison, the original rational-prime action
$\alpha_pf(x)=f(p^{-1}x)$ scales all places. The product formula
makes it an isometry of the global $L^2$ space. On an original
unramified coefficient $f=\xi_p\otimes g$, however,
\[
 Q_p\alpha_pf=
 (1_{p\Z_p}-p^{-1}\xi_p)\otimes\alpha_p^{(p)}g,
 \tag{MQ28}\label{mq:eq:prime-defect}
\]
since $P_p1_{p\Z_p}=p^{-1}\xi_p$. Its squared norm is
\[
 (p^{-1}-p^{-2})\|\alpha_p^{(p)}g\|^2
       =(1-p^{-1})\|g\|^2.
 \tag{MQ29}\label{mq:eq:defect-norm}
\]
The other local moduli have product $p$, proving the second
equality. Equations (CCF33) and (CCF34) are thereby verified with their original
normalizations. At $\ell\ne p$ the rational $p$ is a local
unit and preserves the unramified line there. In a crossed-product
stalk $B_p$ all its original labels are units at $p$; conjugation
by $U_p$ leaves those labels unchanged and gives exactly this
coefficient defect. Thus no endomorphism of the $p$-marked
quotient is inferred from a map that fails to preserve its kernel.
The actual defect map and its positive norm remain specified.

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
 D_W \arrow[r] \arrow[d,hook]
 & D_W/\delta W \arrow[d,hook] \\
 \overline D_W \arrow[r]
 & \mathscr M_W
\end{tikzcd}
\caption{The exact positive arithmetic receiver. The upper map is
the canonical relative arithmetic image in
Proposition~\ref{mq:prop:arithmetic-image}; the lower map subtracts
the arithmetic mean in every marked channel. Its kernel is the
closed diagonal of Theorem~\ref{mq:thm:closure}. The right-hand map
is injective because the algebraic marked space meets that closed
diagonal exactly in $\delta W$. Formula \eqref{mq:eq:variance}
retains every marked-prime multiplicity and every rational label.}
\end{figure}

The calculations affirm the claimed arithmetic image and attained
positive realization. The additional analytic distinction is the
proved unboundedness of the generic diagonal and the resulting
difference between specified relative completion norms. Its exact
closed domain, range, adjoint, norm comparison, and common arithmetic
quotient have all been constructed above. No purity or zeta-zero
claim is inferred from these positive receiving spaces alone.


\section{Prime boundary classes in the actual adelic theta receiver}
\label{sec:prime-memory-theta}

The preceding coefficient calculation can be carried through the
original rational-scaling relation and actual theta map. The
source here is Connes \cite{Connes1998}, Section III, equations
(6)--(19): its two moment conditions, weighted periodization norm,
and the factor $|x|^{1/2}$ are all retained. We compute an exact
kernel which would be lost by taking coinvariants before imposing
the two conditions. It lies in the marked relative group (CCF16).
This calculation uses original author TeX, not a PDF extraction.

Connes--Consani \cite{ConnesConsaniHochschild},
\texttt{h0coinv}, \texttt{propmapE}, and \texttt{propmapE1},
already prove the unconditioned spherical coinvariant and trace
comparison. The calculations below reprove its maps and compute
the extra moment-first kernel and its receiving relative classes.
Their \texttt{quotbruhatschwartz} supplies the real Laplacian
context, which is applied with its exact range at the end.

\subsection{The entire spherical coefficient module}

Write $K=\widehat{\mathbb Z}^{\times}$, $\Gamma=\mathbb Q^\times$,
$\Gamma_+=\mathbb Q_{>0}^{\times}$, and
$R=\mathbb C[\Gamma_+]$. A group-algebra basis element is denoted
$t_a$ for $a\in\Gamma_+$. Let $I=\ker(\operatorname{aug}:R\to\mathbb C)$.
For $a>0$ rational set
\[
 \eta_a=\bigotimes_p1_{p^{v_p(a)}\mathbb Z_p},\qquad
 (\alpha_a f)(x)=f(a^{-1}x),\qquad
 s(h)=\eta_1\otimes h.
 \tag{PM1}
\]
The local action and all rational coefficients are original ones.
Every $\mathbb Z_p^\times$-invariant local Schwartz function is
a unique finite linear combination of $1_{p^n\mathbb Z_p}$,
$n\in\mathbb Z$. In fact its values on valuation shells are
zero below a finite bound and constant above a finite bound;
successive differences give the coefficients of these nested
balls. Their independence follows by evaluating on successive
shells. Averaging a finite tensor over the compact unit groups
is still a finite tensor sum; only finitely many nonstandard
places occur. The global invariant space is therefore
\[
 V^K=\bigoplus_{a\in\Gamma_+}\eta_a\otimes\mathcal S(\mathbb R),
 \quad
 \mathcal J:R\otimes\mathcal S(\mathbb R)\xrightarrow{\sim}V^K,
 \qquad t_a\otimes h\longmapsto\alpha_a s(h).
 \tag{PM2}
\]
Indeed $\alpha_as(h)=\eta_a\otimes h(x/a)$, so the change
between the two displayed coordinates is invertible on every
summand. Rational positive scaling acts on $R$ by left
multiplication and leaves the second coordinate unchanged.
The element $-1\in\Gamma$ acts by $h(x)\mapsto h(-x)$.

Both moments of $\alpha_as(h)$ equal those of $h$. For the
integral this is the exact product $a\prod_p p^{-v_p(a)}=1$,
and evaluation at zero is unchanged. Consequently the moment
map on (PM2) is $\operatorname{aug}\otimes m$, where
\[
 m(h)=\left(h(0),\int_{\mathbb R}h(x)\,dx\right),\quad
 H=\mathcal S(\mathbb R)^{\rm even},\quad H_0=\ker(m|_H),
 \quad M_0=(V^K)^0.
 \tag{PM3}
\]
The superscript zero means both original moment conditions.
The odd functions have both moments zero and vanish under
$\{\pm1\}$ coinvariants: $h-(-h)=2h$. Averaging by
$\tfrac12(1+\alpha_{-1})$ is an exact projection, so all
coinvariant calculations reduce to the even summand without
losing a remaining odd class.

\subsection{Taking the moments first leaves two classes per prime}

Let $\Lambda=\Gamma_+\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb C
=\bigoplus_p\mathbb C\ell_p$, where $\ell_p$ records the
original rational prime generator. There is a canonical split
exact sequence of complex vector spaces
\[
 0\longrightarrow\Lambda\otimes\mathbb C^2
   \xrightarrow{\ b\ }(M_0)_\Gamma
   \xrightarrow{\ \Phi\ }H_0\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{PM4}
\]
The section is the actual unramified source $h\mapsto[s(h)]$.
In the original finite-place coordinates, the quotient map is
\[
 \Phi\left[\sum_a\eta_a\otimes h_a\right](x)
       =\frac12\sum_a\bigl(h_a(ax)+h_a(-ax)\bigr).
 \tag{PM5}
\]
The sum is finite. For $u\in\mathbb C^2$, choose any even
Schwartz function $h$ with $m(h)=u$. Then
\[
 b(\ell_p\otimes u)=[(\alpha_p-1)s(h)].
 \tag{PM6}
\]
The vector in brackets is in $M_0$ although $s(h)$ itself need
not satisfy the moments. Formula (PM6) is independent of the
lift: a difference of lifts belongs to $H_0$, so its difference
under $\alpha_p-1$ is one of the relations defining $(M_0)_\Gamma$.

Here is a full proof of exactness, including its injectivity.
The two even functions
\[
 h_0(x)=(1-2\pi x^2)e^{-\pi x^2},\qquad
 h_1(x)=2\pi x^2e^{-\pi x^2}
 \tag{PM7}
\]
have moments $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$. Thus $H=H_0\oplus
\operatorname{span}\{h_0,h_1\}$. In the even part of (PM2),
the moment kernel is exactly
\[
 R\otimes H_0\ \oplus\ I\otimes\mathbb C^2.
 \tag{PM8}
\]
Positive-rational coinvariants quotient a module by $I$ times
that module. They therefore turn (PM8) into
$H_0\oplus(I/I^2)\otimes\mathbb C^2$.
The canonical map $I/I^2\to\Lambda$ sends $t_a-1$ to
$\sum_pv_p(a)\ell_p$. Products satisfy
$t_{ab}-1=(t_a-1)+(t_b-1)+(t_a-1)(t_b-1)$, and
$t_{p^{-1}}-1\equiv-(t_p-1)\pmod{I^2}$. Hence the classes
$t_p-1$ span. Their independence follows by the derivations
$D_p(t_a)=v_p(a)$: each $D_p$ vanishes on $I^2$ and takes
value $\delta_{pq}$ on $t_q-1$. This proves $I/I^2\simeq\Lambda$.
It proves (PM4) and every map (PM5)--(PM6). Although (PM7)
was used to verify exactness, the resulting injection is
independent of those lifts and the section is already canonical.

The same calculation specifies the higher algebraic group
homology:
\[
 H_n(\Gamma,M_0)\simeq
       \bigwedge^{n+1}\Lambda\otimes\mathbb C^2
       \qquad(n\ge1).
 \tag{PM9}
\]
For completeness, on any finitely generated subgroup of
$\Gamma_+$ use the tensor product of the exact two-term
resolutions with differential $t_p-1$ for its prime generators.
The one-variable resolution is exact because multiplication
by $t_p-1$ is injective on its Laurent polynomial ring and
its quotient is evaluation at one. Tensoring over $\mathbb C$
preserves exactness, giving the finite Koszul resolution.
Tensoring this resolution with a trivial module annihilates
every differential, yielding the exterior powers of its
generator space. Increasing the finite set of generators
and taking the exact filtered union gives
$H_j(\Gamma_+,\mathbb C^2)=\bigwedge^j\Lambda\otimes\mathbb C^2$.
The even $V^K$ in (PM2) is a free $R$ module and hence has
zero higher group homology. The order-two factor has an exact
averaging projection over $\mathbb C$, so adds no higher group.
The long exact sequence of
$0\to M_0\to V^K\to\mathbb C^2\to0$ now proves (PM9),
and its degree-zero portion is exactly (PM4).
These are algebraic homology statements; no Hilbert completion
is hidden in them.

\subsection{The complete map to Connes's periodization}

On the trivial compact-character sector, represent an idele
class by real $x>0$ and finite components one. The original
periodization is
\[
 (\mathcal E f)(x)=x^{1/2}\sum_{q\in\mathbb Q^\times}f(qx).
 \tag{PM10}
\]
It is invariant under $\alpha_q$, by reindexing the sum. On
an unramified even source the finite conditions force
$q\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$, and therefore
\[
 \mathcal E f(x)=x^{1/2}\Theta\Phi[f](x),\qquad
 \Theta h(x)=2\sum_{n\ge1}h(nx).
 \tag{PM11}
\]
This holds first on each summand $\alpha_a s(h)$ of (PM2)
and then by linearity. The factor two is the sum over positive
and negative integers; the factor $x^{1/2}$ is the source's
specified measure comparison, not a new convention.

The map $\Theta$ is injective on $H$. Indeed, for fixed $x>0$,
rapid decay implies absolute convergence of
$\sum_{m,n\ge1}|h(mnx)|$: for $A>2$ it is bounded by
a constant times $x^{-A}\zeta(A)^2$. With the integer Möbius
function $\mu$, the exact divisor identity
$\sum_{d\mid r}\mu(d)=1$ for $r=1$ and zero otherwise gives
\[
 h(x)=\frac12\sum_{n\ge1}\mu(n)\Theta h(nx).
 \tag{PM12}
\]
The divisor identity follows by expanding
$\prod_{p\mid r}(1-1)$ for $r>1$ and its empty product for
$r=1$. This supplies a direct proof of injectivity with no
assumption about zeta zeros. Continuity gives $h(0)$ as well,
and evenness determines negative arguments.

For $h\in H_0$, both $h(0)$ and $\widehat h(0)=\int h$ vanish.
Poisson summation gives
\[
 \Theta h(x)=x^{-1}\Theta\widehat h(1/x).
 \tag{PM13}
\]
One proof in these exact conventions is to periodize
$t\mapsto h(xt)$ on $\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$. Its uniformly
convergent Fourier series has coefficients
$x^{-1}\widehat h(n/x)$; evaluation at zero gives the equality
of the sums over all integers. Both zero-index terms vanish,
which gives (PM13). All derivatives are controlled by the
same Schwartz bounds. Thus $\Theta h$ decays to arbitrary
order at infinity and, by (PM13), at zero after multiplication
by any fixed power. In particular the original norm
\[
 \|f\|_\delta^2=
   \int_0^\infty |\Theta\Phi[f](x)|^2
          (1+\log^2x)^{\delta/2}\,x\,\frac{dx}{x}
 \quad(\delta>1)
 \tag{PM14}
\]
is finite. It is exactly Connes's norm in this sector, with
its original weight. Equation (PM12) proves the exact kernel
after algebraic coinvariants:
\[
 \ker\bigl(\mathcal E:(M_0)_\Gamma\to
               C^\infty(\mathbb R_{>0})\bigr)
       =b(\Lambda\otimes\mathbb C^2).
 \tag{PM15}
\]
The same kernel is killed by (PM14). Consequently the
moment-first quotient contains every prime boundary class,
while the original periodization seminorm explicitly removes
them. This is a calculated kernel with its unchanged
representatives, rather than an identification of those classes
with absent $\tau$.

\subsection{Their actual support map, scaling characters, and Fourier action}

Use the invariant marked relative space $D^K=D_V^K$ from
(CCF10). Unit averaging commutes with its projections, and
finite averaging on every local test proves exactness of
taking these invariants. Let
\[
 R_0=\operatorname{span}\{(\alpha_q-1)f:
            q\in\Gamma,\ f\in M_0\},\qquad
 D_{\Gamma,0}=D^K/\delta_VR_0.
 \tag{PM16}
\]
This is the stated quotient of the original relative group;
no action on $D^K$ is presumed when a rational prime fails
to preserve a local unramified line. The map $\delta_V$
nevertheless induces an exact injection
\[
 \Lambda\otimes\mathbb C^2\hookrightarrow D_{\Gamma,0},
 \qquad \ell_p\otimes u\longmapsto
             [\delta_V((\alpha_p-1)s(h))].
 \tag{PM17}
\]
The only nonzero marked component of this representative is
at $p$, where it is $[\alpha_ps(h)]_p$, the actual defect
(CCF33). Other primes see a unit and retain the standard
factor, so their quotient class is zero. This connects the
new classes to the original prime, not to an auxiliary index.

To prove injectivity of (PM17), suppose a representative
$v\in M_0$ of a class in the kernel of $\Phi$ satisfies
$\delta_Vv\in\delta_VR_0$. Then $v-r\in\ker\delta_V$ for
some $r\in R_0$. By (CCF11) its value is $s(h)$ with
$h\in\mathcal S(\mathbb R)$; since $v,r$ have both moments
zero, $h$ has both moments zero. In coinvariants its class
is $[s(h^{\rm even})]$, whose image under $\Phi$ is
$h^{\rm even}$. The class under consideration had image
zero, so $h^{\rm even}=0$. Its odd part is already an
order-two relation. Therefore $[v]=0$ in $(M_0)_\Gamma$,
which proves injectivity. The image of the full induced map
$(M_0)_\Gamma\to D_{\Gamma,0}$ is precisely (PM17), since
the unramified section of $H_0$ has $\delta_Vs(h)=0$.

Archimedean scaling commutes with every rational action and
with $\delta_V$. It therefore acts on all these groups.
Its exact action on the new prime classes is
\[
 \vartheta_\lambda b(\ell_p\otimes(u_0,u_1))
      =b(\ell_p\otimes(u_0,\lambda u_1)).
 \tag{PM18}
\]
Apply (CCF21) to the chosen lift $h$; (PM6) is independent of
the remaining zero-moment difference. This proves (PM18)
without choosing homogeneous Schwartz lifts. The section
$H_0\to(M_0)_\Gamma$ also commutes with $\vartheta_\lambda$,
so (PM4) is equivariantly split for this action.

Fourier inversion on the original adelic space satisfies
$\mathcal F\alpha_p=\alpha_{p^{-1}}\mathcal F$ and
$\mathcal Fs(h)=s(\widehat h)$. It induces a map on
$(M_0)_\Gamma$. In $I/I^2$ inversion sends $t_p-1$ to its
negative, while Fourier exchanges the two moments. Hence
\[
 \mathcal F b(\ell_p\otimes(u_0,u_1))
       =-b(\ell_p\otimes(u_1,u_0)).
 \tag{PM19}
\]
One may verify the sign directly by
$t_p^{-1}-1=-t_p^{-1}(t_p-1)$ and reducing modulo $I^2$.
Moreover $\Phi\mathcal F=\widehat{\phantom h}\,\Phi$,
by (PM2) and the same inversion of rational labels.
Together (PM13), (PM18), and (PM19) give the exact reflection
and both pole characters before and after periodization.
For example
$\mathcal E(\mathcal F f)(x)=\mathcal E f(1/x)$
for $f\in M_0$, including the retained factor $x^{1/2}$.

Finally the map to the original arithmetic image (CCF19)
sends the representatives (PM17) to zero because they are
in $\delta_VV$. Thus the complete receiving diagram has
three specified operations: passage to the split relative
group, rational-scaling relations imposed after the moment
conditions, and the arithmetic or theta receiver. The new
prime classes survive the first two operations as (PM17)
and have the exactly computed kernel in the third. Their
survival in the relative space and their removal by the
periodization norm are both part of the reconstructed
mathematics.

\subsection{The Laplacian range and the full missing moment-first module}

Retain the human source's differential operator
$H=x\partial_x$ and $\Delta=H(1+H)$, acting at the real
coordinate. The map $\Delta:H\to H_0$ is an isomorphism
\cite[\texttt{quotbruhatschwartz}]{ConnesConsaniHochschild};
in this sentence the first $H$ denotes the space of even
Schwartz functions from (PM3), and in the formulas below we
write that space explicitly to avoid confusion with the operator.
Here is a direct inverse in the original coordinate. For
$f\in H_0$ and $x>0$ set
\[
 u(x)=-\int_x^\infty\frac{f(t)}t\,dt,
 \qquad g(x)=-\frac1x\int_x^\infty u(t)\,dt.
 \tag{PM20}
\]
Extend both evenly. Since $f$ is even and $f(0)=0$,
$f(t)/t$ is smooth odd and vanishes to first order at zero.
Its primitive $u$ is smooth even and rapidly decreasing at
infinity. It obeys $Hu=f$. Integration by parts gives
$\int_0^\infty u=-\int_0^\infty f=0$. Thus near zero the
second expression equals $x^{-1}\int_0^xu(t)dt$, which is
smooth even; its expression as a tail proves rapid decay.
It satisfies $(1+H)g=u$, hence $\Delta g=f$.
The kernel of $H$ on Schwartz functions is zero, and the
kernel of $1+H$ there is zero as well, since its solutions
have $xg$ constant away from zero. This proves injectivity
and the full inverse claim.

In the even spherical adelic coordinates (PM2), $\Delta$
acts as $1\otimes\Delta$; it commutes with every rational
scaling. It follows that its entire range is $R\otimes H_0$.
The exact extra quotient before taking coinvariants is
\[
 M_0^{\rm even}/\Delta(V^{K,\rm even})
       \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } I\otimes\mathbb C^2,
 \qquad \sum_at_a\otimes h_a\longmapsto
                         \sum_at_a\otimes m(h_a).
 \tag{PM21}
\]
The moment condition says exactly that the target has
augmentation zero. The kernel is $R\otimes H_0$, and
surjectivity follows using (PM7) coefficientwise. After
coinvariants the exact remaining quotient is
$(I/I^2)\otimes\mathbb C^2$, namely (PM4)'s prime classes.
Thus the real Laplacian theorem and the full adelic
moment-first space have an explicit comparison with a
calculated cokernel. The original theta image is unchanged
by this cokernel, because (PM15) is exactly its kernel.

On the positive arithmetic Hilbert quotient (CCF31)--(CCF32),
the same original operator has a fully determined realization.
On finite Schwartz channels integration by parts gives
\[
 H^*=-1-H,\qquad
 \Delta=-H^*H=(H+\tfrac12)^2-\tfrac14,
 \qquad
 \langle f,\Delta f\rangle
       =-\|(H+\tfrac12)f\|^2-\tfrac14\|f\|^2.
 \tag{PM22}
\]
The boundary terms $x\overline f g$ vanish at zero and
infinity. Each finite-place projection and marked mean
commutes with $H$, so the same calculation holds on all
zero-sum marked channels. This proves the exact sign and
the retained $1/4$ on the actual attained receiver.

Its closed realization and spectrum can also be calculated.
On the positive and negative half-lines, respectively, the maps
$f\mapsto e^{t/2}f(e^t)$ and $f\mapsto e^{t/2}f(-e^t)$
are unitary from additive $L^2$ to $L^2(\mathbb R,dt)$.
They send $H+1/2$ to $\partial_t$. The logarithmic Fourier transform here is explicitly
$(2\pi)^{-1/2}\int e^{-it\xi}h(t)\,dt$; it therefore sends $\Delta$ to multiplication by
$-\xi^2-1/4$, on its exact domain
\[
 \{h\in L^2(\mathbb R):
              (\xi^2+1/4)h(\xi)\in L^2(\mathbb R)\}.
 \tag{PM23}
\]
Real multiplication on this domain is selfadjoint: its
resolvents at nonreal parameters are the bounded multipliers
$(-\xi^2-1/4-z)^{-1}$, and the adjoint condition obtained by
testing compactly supported functions gives exactly the same
domain. Compact smooth functions away from $x=0$, in each
half-line and each finite channel, give a core by truncation
and smooth approximation in the multiplication graph norm.
Consequently its spectrum on any nonzero such arithmetic
channel is $(-\infty,-1/4]$; approximate functions supported
near a frequency show every value in that interval occurs
in the spectrum, while outside it the reciprocal is bounded.
Each level set has measure zero, so there is no nonzero
eigenvector at a single spectral value. For an orthogonal sum, its domain additionally requires the sum
of the integrals $\int(\xi^2+1/4)^2|h_a(\xi)|^2d\xi$ over all
channels to be finite. This follows directly by applying the
multiplication operator to the whole Hilbert direct sum. Its spectrum
is the same interval when at least one channel is nonzero.
This is a complete spectral calculation for the specified
adelic Haar receiver. The source's full zeta-jet receiver
and this Hilbert receiver remain connected by the explicit
source maps and kernels (PM4)--(PM21); no identification of
their spectral quotients is assumed.

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
0\arrow[r]&\Lambda\otimes\mathbb C^2\arrow[r,"b"]\arrow[d,hook]
 &(M_0)_\Gamma\arrow[r,"\Phi"]\arrow[d,"\delta_V"]
 &H_0\arrow[r]\arrow[d,hook,"x^{1/2}\Theta"]&0\\
 &D_{\Gamma,0}&D_{\Gamma,0}
 &C^\infty(\mathbb R_{>0})
\end{tikzcd}
\caption{The entire moment-first kernel of the original theta
receiver. The top row is (PM4), with canonical unramified
section. The first two vertical maps agree on $b$ by (PM17);
the right vertical map is injective by (PM12). The composite
along the top and right is the actual periodization (PM10).
Each prime contributes both pole characters, with Fourier
sign and exchange as in (PM19). Source of the periodization
and weight: Connes \cite{Connes1998}, Section III (6)--(19).}
\end{figure}

\section{All moment-first classes, higher cycles, and their actual completions}


\subsection{Original source and coefficient coordinates}

The source is Alain Connes, \emph{Trace formula in noncommutative
Geometry and the zeros of the Riemann zeta function},
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/math/9811068}{arXiv:math/9811068}.
The supplied original author file \path{math_9811068.tex} was
read at lines 646--825: Section III, equations (6)--(19), including
the two moments, the weighted norm, its Haar normalization, the
periodization factor, and its exact scaling identity. This is an
independent derivation of (PM1)--(PM19) of the programme source
\path{PRIME_MEMORY_AND_ACTUAL_THETA_RECEIVER.tex}.
The new maps below also identify precisely what happens in the
marked Hilbert receiver, rather than identifying algebraic
coinvariants with a completed quotient.

Put $V=\mathcal S(\mathbb A_\Q)$, $K=\widehat\Z^{\times}$,
$\Gamma=\Q^{\times}$, $\Gamma_+=\Q_{>0}^{\times}$, and
$R=\C[\Gamma_+]$. Its basis is $t_a$ for positive rational $a$,
and $I=\ker\aug$ is its augmentation ideal. Use the actual action
and the actual unramified section
\[
 \alpha_af(x)=f(a^{-1}x),\qquad
 s(h)=h\otimes\bigotimes_p1_{\Z_p},\qquad
 \eta_a=\bigotimes_p1_{p^{v_p(a)}\Z_p}.
 \tag{MC1}\label{mc:eq:coordinates}
\]
Every $\Z_p^{\times}$-invariant local test is a unique finite
linear combination of $1_{p^n\Z_p}$, $n\in\Z$. Its shell values
vanish for sufficiently negative valuations and become constant
for sufficiently positive valuations; successive differences give
exactly the claimed coefficients. Independence follows by testing
successive shells. Expanding finite adelic tensors therefore gives
the exact isomorphism
\[
 J:R\otimes\mathcal S(\mathbb R)\xrightarrow{\sim}V^K,
 \qquad J(t_a\otimes h)=\alpha_as(h)
       =\eta_a\otimes h(x/a).
 \tag{MC2}\label{mc:eq:free-module}
\]
Compact-unit averaging is valid here without an integration
completion: a local test has an open stabilizer in its compact
unit group, so its orbit is finite. Every relevant averaging is
a finite average and remains an original adelic test. This also
proves exactness of invariants on the modules and marked quotients
used below. Positive rationals act on the first factor of
\eqref{mc:eq:free-module} by left multiplication. The element $-1$
acts on the second factor by real reflection.

Let
\[
 \begin{gathered} m(h)=\left(h(0),\int_{\mathbb R}h(x)\,dx\right),\\
 H=\mathcal S(\mathbb R)^{\mathrm{even}},\qquad H_0=\ker(m|_H),
 \quad M_0=\ker(m_{\mathbb A}:V^K\to\C^2).
 \end{gathered}\tag{MC3}\label{mc:eq:moments}
\]
Both adelic moments of $\alpha_as(h)$ equal $m(h)$: evaluation
is fixed, and the integral scales by
$a\prod_p p^{-v_p(a)}=1$. Hence the moment map in
\eqref{mc:eq:free-module} is exactly $\aug\otimes m$.
Odd functions already have both moments zero. Their coinvariant
classes vanish because reflection acts by $-1$ and the relation
is twice the vector. Averaging by $\tfrac12(1+\alpha_{-1})$ is
an exact projection, so the even reduction loses no surviving
coinvariant class.

\subsection{All moment-first coinvariants and their explicit inverse coordinates}

Write
\[
 \Lambda=\bigoplus_p\C\ell_p,
 \qquad \ell(a)=\sum_pv_p(a)\ell_p.
 \tag{MC4}\label{mc:eq:prime-vectors}
\]
The sums are finite, with the original prime exponents and signs.
Let $R_0$ be the span of $(\alpha_q-1)f$ with $q\in\Gamma$ and
$f\in M_0$. Then $(M_0)_\Gamma=M_0/R_0$.

\begin{theorem}[The exact canonical split sequence]
\label{mc:thm:coinvariants}
There is a canonical split exact sequence
\[
 0\longrightarrow\Lambda\otimes\C^2
 \xrightarrow{b}(M_0)_\Gamma
 \xrightarrow{\Phi}H_0\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{MC5}\label{mc:eq:coinvariant-sequence}
\]
Its section is $h\mapsto[s(h)]$, and its injection is
\[
 b(\ell_p\otimes u)=[(\alpha_p-1)s(h)]
 \quad\text{for any }h\in H\text{ with }m(h)=u.
 \tag{MC6}\label{mc:eq:boundary-class}
\]
For a representative $f=\sum_a\alpha_as(h_a)\in M_0$, its
complete inverse coordinates are
\[
 \Phi[f]=\sum_a h_a^{\mathrm{even}},\qquad
 \beta[f]=\sum_a\ell(a)\otimes m(h_a),
 \qquad
 [f]=[s(\Phi[f])]+b(\beta[f]).
 \tag{MC7}\label{mc:eq:inverse-coordinates}
\]
In the original coordinates $f=\sum_a\eta_a\otimes g_a$,
the first formula is
$\Phi[f](x)=\tfrac12\sum_a(g_a(ax)+g_a(-ax))$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The even functions
\[
 u(x)=(1-2\pi x^2)e^{-\pi x^2},\qquad
 v(x)=2\pi x^2e^{-\pi x^2}
 \tag{MC8}\label{mc:eq:moment-lifts}
\]
have moments $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$, because
$\int e^{-\pi x^2}=1$ and $\int x^2e^{-\pi x^2}=1/(2\pi)$.
Thus $H=H_0\oplus\C u\oplus\C v$. The even moment kernel is
exactly the $R$-module
\[
 R\otimes H_0\ \oplus\ I\otimes\C^2.
 \tag{MC9}\label{mc:eq:moment-module}
\]
Taking positive-rational coinvariants quotients by $I$ times this
module and gives $H_0\oplus(I/I^2)\otimes\C^2$.
The map $t_a-1\mapsto\ell(a)$ identifies $I/I^2$ with
$\Lambda$. Indeed
$t_{ab}-1=(t_a-1)+(t_b-1)+(t_a-1)(t_b-1)$ and
$t_{p^{-1}}-1\equiv-(t_p-1)\pmod{I^2}$ show spanning by
the prime classes. The linear derivation $D_p(t_a)=v_p(a)$
vanishes on $I^2$ and takes value $\delta_{pq}$ on $t_q-1$,
proving independence. This proves the exact sequence.

Changing the lift in \eqref{mc:eq:boundary-class} changes it by
$(\alpha_p-1)s(h_0)$ with $h_0\in H_0$, an actual relation in
$R_0$. Thus $b$ is independent of the moment lifts.
The first formula of \eqref{mc:eq:inverse-coordinates} is augmentation
followed by even projection. Its moments are zero because $f\in M_0$.
For the second, applying $(\alpha_c-1)$ to a representative changes
the indicated sum by
$\ell(c)\otimes\sum_am(h_a)=0$. Reflection also leaves it
unchanged, since both moments of an odd function vanish. Thus
$\beta$ is defined on the stated coinvariants. It is zero on the
section $s(H_0)$ and takes $b(\ell_p\otimes u)$ to
$\ell_p\otimes u$. The just-proved direct sum therefore proves
the final equality and all canonicity claims.
\end{proof}

For comparison, quotienting $V^K$ by rational relations first gives
$H$; imposing its two moments then gives only $H_0$. The precise
defect is the kernel in \eqref{mc:eq:coinvariant-sequence}. More
explicitly, if $I_\Gamma V^K$ denotes the span of all
$(\alpha_q-1)f$ with $f\in V^K$, then its moments vanish and
\[
 I_\Gamma V^K/I_\Gamma M_0\simeq\Lambda\otimes\C^2,
 \qquad I_\Gamma M_0=R_0.
 \tag{MC10}\label{mc:eq:order-defect}
\]
This follows because augmentation is the complete quotient
$V^K\to H$ and its restriction to $M_0$ is $\Phi$.

\subsection{Higher pole classes with explicit cycles and actions}

\begin{theorem}[All higher algebraic group homology]
\label{mc:thm:homology}
For $n\ge1$ there is the canonical connecting isomorphism
\[
 H_n(\Gamma,M_0)\simeq
       \bigwedge^{n+1}\Lambda\otimes\C^2.
 \tag{MC11}\label{mc:eq:higher-homology}
\]
For distinct ordered primes $p_0,\ldots,p_n$ and a lift $h$ of
$u\in\C^2$, the class corresponding to
$\ell_{p_0}\wedge\cdots\wedge\ell_{p_n}\otimes u$ is represented
in the finite-prime Koszul complex by the exact cycle
\[
 \sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^i(\alpha_{p_i}-1)s(h)
       \otimes e_{p_0}\wedge\cdots\widehat{e_{p_i}}
                                      \cdots\wedge e_{p_n}.
 \tag{MC12}\label{mc:eq:explicit-cycles}
\]
The same formula for $n=0$ is \eqref{mc:eq:boundary-class}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For a finite set of prime generators, tensor the two-term Laurent
polynomial resolutions with differential $t_p-1$. Each is exact
because this multiplication is injective and its cokernel is
evaluation at one. Tensor products over the coefficient field
preserve exactness. On a trivial module every differential becomes
zero, so the result in degree $j$ is its tensor with the $j$th
exterior power of the prime-generator space. The subgroups generated
by finite prime sets are cofinal among the finite subsets of
$\Gamma_+$, and every group-homology chain uses finitely many group
elements. Their exact filtered union therefore gives
$H_j(\Gamma_+,\C^2)=\bigwedge^j\Lambda\otimes\C^2$.

The module in \eqref{mc:eq:free-module} is free over $R$, and remains
free when restricted to any such subgroup. Hence its higher
positive-rational homology is zero. The order-two group is exact
under averaging over $\C$, so adds no positive-degree homology.
The long exact sequence of
$0\to M_0\to V^K\to\C^2\to0$ gives
\eqref{mc:eq:higher-homology}. The Koszul differential of
$s(h)\otimes e_{p_0}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{p_n}$ is exactly
\eqref{mc:eq:explicit-cycles}. Each coefficient has both moments zero,
and its next differential is zero because the Koszul differential
squares to zero. Changing $h$ by a zero-moment lift changes the
cycle by a boundary in the $M_0$ complex itself. This verifies the
stated cycle and its independence without an implicit lift choice.
\end{proof}

Let $\vartheta_\lambda$ scale the real coordinate by
$f(x_\infty,x_f)\mapsto f(\lambda^{-1}x_\infty,x_f)$.
It commutes with rational scaling and acts on the two moments by
$\operatorname{diag}(1,\lambda)$. Thus it acts on each right-hand
side of \eqref{mc:eq:coinvariant-sequence} and
\eqref{mc:eq:higher-homology} by the identity on the exterior prime
factor and that exact two-character action on $\C^2$.
The adelic Fourier transform satisfies
$\mathcal F\alpha_a=\alpha_{a^{-1}}\mathcal F$ and
$\mathcal Fs(h)=s(\widehat h)$. It exchanges the two moments.
Inversion on $\Gamma_+$ negates each generator of $\Lambda$.
Consequently its exact action on the higher prime module is
\[
 \mathcal F(\omega\otimes(u_0,u_1))
       =(-1)^{n+1}\omega\otimes(u_1,u_0)
 \quad\text{in degree }n\ge0.
 \tag{MC13}\label{mc:eq:higher-fourier}
\]
One can also check the sign on chains: inversion sends the Koszul
basis $e_p$ to $-t_p^{-1}e_p$, since
$t_p^{-1}-1=-t_p^{-1}(t_p-1)$. On trivial coefficients this is
exactly the stated exterior sign. Degree zero gives (PM19).

\subsection{The original periodization and its complete kernel}

In the compact-unit invariant sector an idele class has representative
with real coordinate $x>0$ and finite coordinates one. Connes's
actual map is
\[
 (\mathcal Ef)(x)=x^{1/2}\sum_{q\in\Q^\times}f(qx).
 \tag{MC14}\label{mc:eq:periodization}
\]
For $\alpha_as(h)$ the finite conditions force $q/a\in\Z$, so
the nonzero rational sum is precisely the sum of $h(nx)$ over
nonzero integers $n$. In particular it is absolutely convergent
for each $x>0$. By the finite expansion \eqref{mc:eq:free-module},
all of \eqref{mc:eq:periodization} is therefore
\[
 \mathcal Ef(x)=x^{1/2}\Theta\Phi[f](x),\qquad
 \Theta h(x)=2\sum_{n\ge1}h(nx).
 \tag{MC15}\label{mc:eq:theta}
\]
Reindexing its absolutely convergent rational sum proves invariance
under rational scaling. The two retained constants, $2$ and
$x^{1/2}$, have their original sources: sign summation and comparison
of the multiplicative and weighted measures.

The theta map on $H$ is injective. For fixed $x>0$ and $A>2$,
Schwartz decay bounds the double absolute sum
$\sum_{m,n\ge1}|h(mnx)|$ by $C_Ax^{-A}\zeta(A)^2$. Thus the
integer M\"obius identity can be rearranged to give
\[
 h(x)=\frac12\sum_{n\ge1}\mu(n)\Theta h(nx).
 \tag{MC16}\label{mc:eq:mobius}
\]
Indeed the coefficient of $h(rx)$ is $\sum_{n\mid r}\mu(n)$,
equal to one for $r=1$ and zero otherwise by expanding the product
$\prod_{p\mid r}(1-1)$. Continuity determines $h(0)$ and evenness
determines its negative arguments. This proves injectivity.

For $h\in H_0$, periodizing the Schwartz function $t\mapsto h(xt)$
on $\mathbb R/\Z$ gives a uniformly convergent Fourier series
with coefficients $x^{-1}\widehat h(n/x)$. Evaluation at zero
therefore yields Poisson summation. Both zero-index terms vanish
because both moments vanish, and hence
\[
 \Theta h(x)=x^{-1}\Theta\widehat h(1/x).
 \tag{MC17}\label{mc:eq:poisson}
\]
Schwartz bounds at infinity and this exact reflection imply decay
to every positive order at both endpoints, even after multiplying
by a fixed power. Thus the original Section III(7) norm in this
sector is finite and is exactly
\[
 \|f\|_\delta^2=
 \int_0^\infty|\Theta\Phi[f](x)|^2
        (1+\log^2x)^{\delta/2}\,x\,\frac{dx}{x},
 \qquad\delta>1.
 \tag{MC18}\label{mc:eq:original-norm}
\]
The source's Section III(14) Haar normalization gives compact-unit
volume one, so there is no extra compact-volume multiplier here.
The formula is a seminorm until its exact kernel is removed.
Equations \eqref{mc:eq:coinvariant-sequence}, \eqref{mc:eq:theta}, and
\eqref{mc:eq:mobius} prove
\[
 \ker\bigl(\mathcal E:(M_0)_\Gamma\to
                        C^\infty(\mathbb R_{>0})\bigr)
   =b(\Lambda\otimes\C^2).
 \tag{MC19}\label{mc:eq:theta-kernel}
\]
The same is the exact kernel of \eqref{mc:eq:original-norm}, because
a continuous theta function of zero weighted integral is zero.
Equivalently the kernel on $M_0$ before coinvariants is
$I_\Gamma V^K$, while the coinvariant relations imposed after
the moments are only $I_\Gamma M_0$. This verifies (PM15) with
the two original moments imposed at the stated stage.

\subsection{The full marked extension beyond the injected prime classes}

Use $D=D_V=\bigoplus_p V/V_p$ and $\delta:V\to D$ from
\path{connes_marked_quotient_verification.tex}, (MQ1)--(MQ4).
The compact-unit action preserves every $V_p$, so acts on $D$ and
commutes with $\delta$. Exact finite averaging identifies
$(D/\delta V)^K=D^K/\delta V^K$. Define the exact algebraic
receiver
\[
 D_{\Gamma,0}=D^K/\delta R_0.
 \tag{MC20}\label{mc:eq:marked-coinvariant-receiver}
\]
No rational action on $D$ is assumed in this definition. A rational
prime does not preserve its local unramified line; the formula
uses only the actual images under $\delta$ of existing relations
in $M_0$.

\begin{theorem}[The complete marked extension and all of its maps]
\label{mc:thm:marked-extension}
There is a canonical exact sequence
\[
 0\longrightarrow\Lambda\otimes\C^2
 \xrightarrow{\delta b}D_{\Gamma,0}
 \longrightarrow (D/\delta V)^K\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{MC21}\label{mc:eq:full-marked-extension}
\]
The first map sends $\ell_p\otimes u$ to the class of
$\delta((\alpha_p-1)s(h))$ for any lift $m(h)=u$. Its only
nonzero marked component is the original prime $p$. The right-hand
map is the original arithmetic quotient. The induced map from
$(M_0)_\Gamma$ to $D_{\Gamma,0}$ has kernel exactly $s(H_0)$
and image exactly the displayed prime kernel.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Independence of the moment lift follows because the change belongs
to $\delta R_0$. At a prime $\ell\ne p$, rational $p$ is a
local unit and keeps the factor $1_{\Z_\ell}$, so the marked
class is zero; at $p$ the original standard term is itself zero
in $V/V_p$, leaving $[\alpha_ps(h)]_p$.

Suppose $f\in M_0$ maps to zero in $D_{\Gamma,0}$. Then
$\delta f=\delta r$ for some $r\in R_0$. The kernel of
$\delta$ is the original unramified $s(\mathcal S(\mathbb R))$,
by the arithmetic resolution, so $f-r=s(h)$. Both moments of
$h$ vanish because those of $f$ and $r$ do. Its odd part is a
reflection relation; its even part lies in $H_0$. This proves
that the induced kernel is exactly $s(H_0)$, whose injection
is already proved by $\Phi s=1$. Restricting to $\ker\Phi$
therefore proves injectivity of $\delta b$ and identifies the
whole induced image with the prime module.

Finally $\delta M_0=\delta V^K$. Indeed subtract from any
$f\in V^K$ an unramified $s(h)$ with the same two moments,
using \eqref{mc:eq:moment-lifts}. The difference belongs to $M_0$
and its marked image equals $\delta f$. Therefore the kernel
of the right-hand quotient in \eqref{mc:eq:full-marked-extension}
is exactly $\delta M_0/\delta R_0$, which is the prime image
just computed. Surjectivity and the remaining maps follow from
the displayed successive quotients.
\end{proof}

Thus the injected prime classes are the entire extra kernel over
the original mixed-place arithmetic receiver. All that receiver's
finite-place components, and its real odd coefficients as well,
remain in the right-hand term. Taking $K$ invariants does not
impose real evenness there: the rational relations in this theorem
were imposed on generic coefficient images, not on an assumed
rational action on the marked space.

\subsection{Their exact fate in the inherited marked Hilbert quotient}

Give $D$ the original norm from the orthogonal unramified
projections
$P_pf=1_{\Z_p}\otimes\int_{\Z_p}f(t,\cdot)\,dt$.
Let $\overline D^{,K}$ be the Hilbert completion of $D^K$.
It equals the invariant subspace of $\overline D$: averaging is
a bounded orthogonal projection and preserves the dense algebraic
tests. The marked diagonal closure and its faithful arithmetic
quotient were computed in (MQ15)--(MQ19).

\begin{theorem}[The prime kernel has zero inherited quotient norm]
\label{mc:thm:closure-relations}
In the original marked Hilbert norm,
\[
 \overline{\delta R_0}=\overline{\delta V^K}
          \quad\text{inside }\overline D^{,K}.
 \tag{MC22}\label{mc:eq:relation-closure}
\]
Consequently the separated Hilbert completion of
$D^K/\delta R_0$ in its inherited quotient seminorm is exactly
\[
 \overline D^{,K}/\overline{\delta V^K},
 \tag{MC23}\label{mc:eq:completed-receiver}
\]
the positive arithmetic mixed-place receiver. On its original
algebraic quotient, the radical is exactly
$\delta b(\Lambda\otimes\C^2)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
There is an explicit moment-preserving approximation. For the
functions $u,v$ in \eqref{mc:eq:moment-lifts}, put
\[
 u_n(x)=u(nx),\qquad v_n(x)=n^{-1}v(x/n),\qquad
 h_n=h-h(0)u_n-\left(\int h\right)v_n.
 \tag{MC24}\label{mc:eq:moment-approximation}
\]
For every even Schwartz $h$, the moments of $u_n$ and $v_n$
remain $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$, so $h_n\in H_0$. Moreover
$\|u_n\|_2=n^{-1/2}\|u\|_2$ and
$\|v_n\|_2=n^{-1/2}\|v\|_2$, hence $h_n\to h$ in $L^2$.
All approximants remain original Schwartz functions.

For a fixed prime, the exact defect norm (MQ28)--(MQ29) is
\[
 \bigl\|\delta((\alpha_p-1)s(h))\bigr\|^2
                   =(1-p^{-1})\|h\|_2^2.
 \tag{MC25}\label{mc:eq:boundary-norm}
\]
Thus the same marked vectors with $h_n$ converge to the vector
with $h$, while each approximating vector belongs to $\delta R_0$
because $s(h_n)\in M_0$. Every prime generator of
\eqref{mc:eq:full-marked-extension} therefore lies in
$\overline{\delta R_0}$. The exact sequence and
$\delta M_0=\delta V^K$ imply that every algebraic element of
$\delta V^K$ is a sum of an element of $\delta R_0$ and
finitely many such prime vectors. This proves one inclusion in
\eqref{mc:eq:relation-closure}; the other is immediate.

To identify the radical exactly, the faithful arithmetic quotient
calculation (MQ16)--(MQ18) gives
$D\cap\overline{\delta V}=\delta V$. Averaging shows
$D^K\cap\overline{\delta V^K}=\delta V^K$: an invariant
algebraic vector in the full closure has a lift $f\in V$, and
averaging that lift gives one in $V^K$. Its class modulo
$\delta R_0$ is precisely the kernel in
\eqref{mc:eq:full-marked-extension}. This proves both the exact
radical and \eqref{mc:eq:completed-receiver}.
\end{proof}

The approximation in \eqref{mc:eq:moment-approximation} does not
converge in the Schwartz topology in general: its first correction
concentrates at zero and its second spreads to large scale. It is
therefore not a relation erasing the original algebraic classes.
It proves their fate under the specific inherited $L^2$ quotient
operation. This is distinct from the exact theta kernel, though
both receivers kill the same prime module by the maps just proved.

\subsection{The positive weight constraint and a retained indefinite form}

On the prime module the actual operators are
\[
 T_\lambda(\ell_p\otimes(u_0,u_1))
       =\ell_p\otimes(u_0,\lambda u_1),\qquad
 F(\ell_p\otimes(u_0,u_1))
       =-\ell_p\otimes(u_1,u_0).
 \tag{MC26}\label{mc:eq:prime-operators}
\]
The second sign is the original Fourier inversion of the prime
generator in \eqref{mc:eq:higher-fourier}, not a chosen orientation.

\begin{proposition}[The complete positive obstruction and its receiver]
\label{mc:prop:positive-obstruction}
Every positive semidefinite Hermitian form on $D_{\Gamma,0}$
satisfying the original marked scaling identity
\[
 Q(T_\lambda z,T_\lambda z)=\lambda Q(z,z)
                       \quad(\lambda>0)
 \tag{MC27}\label{mc:eq:weight-identity}
\]
annihilates the entire prime module in
\eqref{mc:eq:full-marked-extension}. Hence it factors through that
actual arithmetic quotient. The inherited marked quotient has
exactly this radical, by Theorem~\ref{mc:thm:closure-relations}.
There is nevertheless an explicit nondegenerate Hermitian form
on the retained algebraic prime module:
\[
 J(z,w)=\sum_p\left(z_{p,0}\overline{w_{p,1}}
                       +z_{p,1}\overline{w_{p,0}}\right).
 \tag{MC28}\label{mc:eq:indefinite-form}
\]
It satisfies $J(T_\lambda z,T_\lambda w)=\lambda J(z,w)$
and $J(Fz,Fw)=J(z,w)$. It has signature $(1,1)$ at every
prime, retains both original characters, and is not a positive
realization.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For a prime vector $z$ with coordinates $(u_0,0)$, its action is
fixed. Equation
\eqref{mc:eq:weight-identity} gives $Q(z,z)=\lambda Q(z,z)$,
so $Q(z,z)=0$ by taking $\lambda\ne1$. For the other coordinate
it gives $\lambda^2Q(z,z)=\lambda Q(z,z)$ and the same
conclusion. The Cauchy--Schwarz inequality for a positive
semidefinite Hermitian form then makes these null vectors
orthogonal to every vector. Finite sums give the full prime
module in the radical, proving factorization through the actual
quotient. No completion assumption is needed for this argument.

In \eqref{mc:eq:indefinite-form}, every vector has finite prime
support, so the sum is defined. Its local matrix is
$\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$,
which is invertible and has eigenvalues $1$ and $-1$.
Substituting \eqref{mc:eq:prime-operators} gives the two asserted
identities term by term; the two Fourier minus signs cancel.
For clarity, the form is an explicit additional construction
from the original moment coordinates. Its constant has not been
inferred from a cohomological degree. The whole family on one
prime compatible with \eqref{mc:eq:weight-identity} has matrix
$\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&c\\\overline c&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$:
the same scaling equation forces both diagonal entries to vanish.
Fourier invariance forces $c$ real, and nondegeneracy is precisely
$c\ne0$. Formula \eqref{mc:eq:indefinite-form} specifies the value
$c=1$ in the displayed original coordinates.
\end{proof}

Completing this last explicitly specified form with the positive
coordinate norm $\sum_p(|z_{p,0}|^2+|z_{p,1}|^2)$ gives
$\ell^2(\{\text{primes}\})\otimes\C^2$, with a bounded
invertible fundamental symmetry given by the same exchange matrix.
This is a concrete indefinite Hilbert-space form retaining the
prime classes. Its scaling has the two actual characters, and its
Fourier operator is the stated negative exchange. It is not the
inherited marked quotient completion \eqref{mc:eq:completed-receiver};
the exact maps and kernels relating that positive completion to
the algebraic objects were proved above.

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
 0\arrow[r]&\Lambda\otimes\C^2\arrow[r,"\delta b"]\arrow[d]
 &D_{\Gamma,0}\arrow[r]\arrow[d]
 &(D/\delta V)^K\arrow[r]\arrow[d,hook]&0\\
 &0\arrow[r]&\overline D^{\,K}/\overline{\delta V^K}
 \arrow[r,equal]&\overline D^{\,K}/\overline{\delta V^K}
\end{tikzcd}
\caption{The exact marked extension and its inherited positive
completion. The top row is Theorem~\ref{mc:thm:marked-extension}.
The explicit Schwartz moment corrections
\eqref{mc:eq:moment-approximation} prove the zero map on the left
and the closure equality in Theorem~\ref{mc:thm:closure-relations}.
The arithmetic right-hand map is injective by the orthogonal
marked-mean quotient calculation. The separate indefinite form
\eqref{mc:eq:indefinite-form} retains the prime classes with their
two original scaling characters and Fourier sign.}
\end{figure}

All moment-first coinvariant, higher homology, and theta-kernel
claims checked here are algebraically valid. Their exact positive
completion has the additional kernel proved above. No spectral
purity or zeta-zero conclusion is inferred from these computations.


\section{The exact Euler domains and their receiving maps}


This independent addendum checks (PM20)--(PM23) in
\path{PRIME_MEMORY_AND_ACTUAL_THETA_RECEIVER.tex}.
It uses the complete module and map calculations in
\path{moment_first_coinvariants_verification.tex},
(MC5)--(MC7), (MC21)--(MC28), and the marked Hilbert receiver
constructed in \path{connes_marked_quotient_verification.tex},
(MQ15)--(MQ18). It specifies the logarithmic Fourier convention
and the full orthogonal-sum domain explicitly. The differential
operator, original real coordinate, moments, and retained constant
$1/4$ are unchanged.

\subsection{The exact real inverse and the full adelic quotient}

Write $\mathscr S=\mathcal S(\mathbb R)^{\mathrm{even}}$,
$m(h)=(h(0),\int h)$, $\mathscr S_0=\ker m$,
$E=x\partial_x$, and $\Delta=E(1+E)$. The letter $E$ in
this addendum denotes the differential operator, not periodization.
First $\Delta\mathscr S\subset\mathscr S_0$: its evaluation at
zero is zero and integration by parts gives
$\int\Delta g=-\int(1+E)g=0$.

\begin{theorem}[The complete inverse]
For $f\in\mathscr S_0$, put for $x>0$
\[
 u(x)=-\int_x^\infty f(t)t^{-1}\,dt,
 \qquad g(x)=-x^{-1}\int_x^\infty u(t)\,dt.
 \tag{LD1}\label{ld:eq:inverse}
\]
Their even extensions are Schwartz functions, and $\Delta g=f$.
This defines the inverse of the continuous linear isomorphism
$\Delta:\mathscr S\to\mathscr S_0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Because $f$ is even and $f(0)=0$, its quotient $f(t)/t$ extends
smoothly and oddly across zero and vanishes there. Differentiating
the first integral gives $u'=f/x$, hence $Eu=f$. Its even extension
is smooth. Its tail and each derivative decay rapidly at infinity.
Fubini is valid since the corresponding double absolute integral
equals $\int_0^\infty|f(t)|dt$, and gives
$\int_0^\infty u=-\int_0^\infty f=0$. Therefore near zero
\[
 g(x)=x^{-1}\int_0^xu(t)\,dt=\int_0^1u(tx)\,dt.
\]
This proves smooth even extension of $g$, including all derivatives.
The tail expression in \eqref{ld:eq:inverse} proves rapid decay of
it and its derivatives. Differentiating $xg$ gives
$(1+E)g=u$ and hence $\Delta g=f$.

The kernel of $E$ in Schwartz space is zero: a solution is constant
on each half-line and rapid decay forces each constant to vanish.
The kernel of $1+E$ is zero too, because $xg$ is constant on each
half-line. Their product therefore has zero kernel on Schwartz
space. This proves the inverse assertion.
For continuity of the inverse, on $0\le t\le1$ use
$|f(t)|\le t^2\sup_{[0,1]}|f''|/2$. The integral defining
$u(0)$ and the derivatives of $u$ are then bounded by finitely
many Schwartz seminorms of $f$. The displayed average for $g$
does the same near zero. On $x\ge1$ the tail formulas and their
derivatives give any requested weighted bound from finitely many
weighted bounds for $f$ and its derivatives. Thus every Schwartz
seminorm of $g$ is controlled by finitely many of those of $f$.
The direct differential operator is continuous, proving the
claimed topological isomorphism as well as its explicit inverse.
\end{proof}

In the original spherical module
$V^{K,\mathrm{even}}=R\otimes\mathscr S$, rational scaling acts
on $R$ and $E$ acts on the second factor: Euler differentiation
commutes with $x\mapsto x/a$. The entire range is therefore
$R\otimes\mathscr S_0$. In the original moment-first module
the exact quotient is
\[
 M_0^{\mathrm{even}}/\Delta(V^{K,\mathrm{even}})
     \xrightarrow{\sim}I\otimes\C^2,
 \qquad\sum_at_a\otimes h_a\longmapsto
                        \sum_at_a\otimes m(h_a).
 \tag{LD2}\label{ld:eq:adelic-cokernel}
\]
The zero total moment is exactly the augmentation-zero condition.
Its kernel and surjectivity follow coefficient by coefficient from
\eqref{ld:eq:inverse} and the two explicit moment lifts in (MC8).
This verifies (PM21) on the full original coefficient module.

The domain of the induced operator on moment-first coinvariants
is different from the domain in \eqref{ld:eq:adelic-cokernel}.
Put $P=\Lambda\otimes\C^2$. The canonical splitting (MC5)--(MC7)
gives the exact operator
\[
 (M_0)_\Gamma=\mathscr S_0\oplus P,
 \qquad \Delta=(\Delta|_{\mathscr S_0})\oplus0.
 \tag{LD3}\label{ld:eq:coinvariant-operator}
\]
Indeed the unramified section commutes with the operator. On a
prime class, applying $\Delta$ gives
$[(\alpha_p-1)s(\Delta h)]=0$, because $\Delta h\in\mathscr S_0$
and this is an actual relation imposed after the moments.
Equivalently $E$ acts by $0$ and $-1$ on the two pole coordinates,
so $E(1+E)$ kills both. The same argument kills every higher pole
homology group (MC11).

Consequently its exact kernel and cokernel are
\[
 \ker\Delta=P,\qquad
 \operatorname{coker}\Delta\simeq\C^2\oplus P.
 \tag{LD4}\label{ld:eq:coinvariant-defects}
\]
For the second equality the actual quotient map on the first
factor is $f\mapsto m(\Delta^{-1}f)$ from $\mathscr S_0$ to
$\C^2$. It is onto by applying $\Delta$ to arbitrary moment
lifts, and its kernel is exactly $\Delta\mathscr S_0$.
This proves every map in \eqref{ld:eq:coinvariant-defects}.

\subsection{The Haar realization, constants, and full domain}

On a Schwartz coefficient in additive real $L^2$, integration
by parts gives the exact formulas
\[
 E^*=-1-E,\qquad
 \Delta=-E^*E=(E+\tfrac12)^2-\tfrac14,
 \qquad
 \langle f,\Delta f\rangle
     =-\|(E+\tfrac12)f\|^2-\tfrac14\|f\|^2.
 \tag{LD5}\label{ld:eq:haar-sign}
\]
The boundary term is $x\overline f g$, which vanishes at zero
and infinity. The middle identity uses the skew adjointness of
$E+1/2$ on this core. Every orthogonal finite-place projection
and every marked arithmetic mean acts on another factor and
commutes with these operations. Thus the identities hold on the
actual zero-sum marked arithmetic channels, including rational
group labels.

On the positive and negative half-lines define respectively
\[
 (U_+f)(t)=e^{t/2}f(e^t),\qquad
 (U_-f)(t)=e^{t/2}f(-e^t).
 \tag{LD6}\label{ld:eq:log-map}
\]
Changing variables gives unitary maps to $L^2(\mathbb R,dt)$.
Direct differentiation sends $E+1/2$ to $\partial_t$.
For the multiplier $-\xi^2-1/4$ the required logarithmic Fourier
convention is explicitly
\[
 (\mathcal F_{\mathrm{ang}}g)(\xi)
    =(2\pi)^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb R}e^{-it\xi}g(t)\,dt.
 \tag{LD7}\label{ld:eq:angular-fourier}
\]
It sends $\partial_t$ to $i\xi$. If instead the earlier real
Fourier convention $\int e^{-2\pi it\xi}g(t)dt$ is retained,
the multiplier is $-4\pi^2\xi^2-1/4$. These are the exact
coordinate change $\xi_{\mathrm{ang}}=2\pi\xi$, not different
operators or a change of the retained constant $1/4$.

Let $\mathscr K_a$ include all finite-place and zero-sum marked
coefficient factors in an actual arithmetic channel. The full
Hilbert sum, after \eqref{ld:eq:log-map}--\eqref{ld:eq:angular-fourier},
has the selfadjoint realization
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 (\Delta h)_{a,\pm}(\xi)=(-\xi^2-\tfrac14)h_{a,\pm}(\xi),\\
 \Dom\Delta=\left\{h:
   \sum_{a,\pm}\int_{\mathbb R}
    (\xi^2+\tfrac14)^2\|h_{a,\pm}(\xi)\|_{\mathscr K_a}^2
           \,d\xi<\infty\right\}.
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{LD8}\label{ld:eq:full-domain}
\]
Here $h$ also belongs to the Hilbert sum itself. For an even
real sector the unitary map from the entire even real $L^2$
space to one copy is $f\mapsto\sqrt2\,e^{t/2}f(e^t)$;
the same multiplier
and weighted domain hold. The summability in
\eqref{ld:eq:full-domain} is essential: membership in each individual
channel's domain does not suffice for an infinite orthogonal sum.

To prove selfadjointness, test the adjoint against compactly
supported frequency functions with finitely many coefficient
channels. The test determines its value to be the same real
multiplier and imposes exactly \eqref{ld:eq:full-domain}.
Conversely every vector in that domain satisfies the adjoint
identity by Cauchy--Schwarz. Its nonreal resolvents are bounded
multipliers $(-\xi^2-1/4-z)^{-1}$, which verifies the entire
resolvent assertion as well. Finite channel truncation and
frequency cutoff approximate every domain vector in graph norm.
Smooth compact frequency approximations then give Schwartz
functions in $t$. Multiplying those by smooth cutoffs tending
to one approximates them with their first two derivatives in
$L^2(dt)$. Their inverse images under \eqref{ld:eq:log-map} are
smooth and compactly supported away from $x=0$. Thus the original
finite Schwartz-channel operator is a core for this realization.

On every nonzero channel, frequencies in arbitrarily small
neighbourhoods of a prescribed $\xi_0$ give approximate
eigenvectors of value $-\xi_0^2-1/4$. Outside
$(-\infty,-1/4]$ the reciprocal multiplier is bounded.
Every level set of the multiplier has measure zero, so no
nonzero Hilbert eigenvector exists. This proves the full spectrum
$(-\infty,-1/4]$ and all domain claims in the stated angular
convention. In particular zero is in its resolvent set.

\subsection{The exact comparison with the retained prime kernel}

The operator identities above do not identify their different
receiving modules. Their actual maps, already proved in (MC21)
and (MC22), give the diagram
\[
 \begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large]
 (M_0)_\Gamma=\mathscr S_0\oplus P
 \arrow[r,"\delta"]\arrow[rr,bend right=20,"0"']
 &D_{\Gamma,0}\arrow[r]
 &\overline D^{\,K}/\overline{\delta V^K}.
 \end{tikzcd}
 \tag{LD9}\label{ld:eq:zero-composite}
\]
The first map has kernel exactly $\mathscr S_0$ and image
exactly $P$. The second map kills exactly $P$ on the algebraic
marked quotient, and its image is the faithfully embedded dense
arithmetic mixed-place quotient. Therefore the displayed composite
is exactly zero. All arrows commute with $E$ and $\Delta$ on
their stated algebraic domains. On the prime module $\Delta=0$;
on the attained positive Haar quotient its closed realization
has the strictly negative spectral gap just proved. This
agreement is accounted for by the exact kernel in
\eqref{ld:eq:zero-composite}. It supplies no identification of the
theta receiver with the Haar arithmetic receiver and no transfer
of a zeta-zero assertion between them.


\section{Full jet duality and all prime-compatible positive forms}


\subsection{The original coefficients and the complete local pairing}
Keep
\[
 g(s)=2\xi(s)=s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s),\qquad
 \iota\rho=1-\bar\rho.
 \tag{FJ1}\label{fj:fj:objects}
\]
Let $Z$ be a nonempty finite set of distinct nontrivial zeros, invariant
under $\iota$. Every multiplicity $m_\rho$ is the actual multiplicity.
Write $t_\rho=s-\rho$ and
\[
 g(\rho+t)=u_\rho(t)t^{m_\rho},\quad
 u_\rho(t)=2\lambda_\rho e^{h_\rho(t)},\quad
 \lambda_\rho=\xi^{(m_\rho)}(\rho)/m_\rho!,\quad h_\rho(0)=0.
 \tag{FJ2}
\]
All equalities below use this coordinate and this unit. The finite algebra is
$A=A_Z=\prod_{\rho\in Z}\mathcal O_\rho/(g)$; its coordinate identification
with $\prod_\rho\C[t_\rho]/(t_\rho^{m_\rho})$ follows because $u_\rho$
is invertible, and does not replace $g$ in any residue.
These are the finite jets in the supplied calculation \cite{FJprime},
Sections 2--4, and the original tau coefficient model \cite{FJtau},
Sections 5--6. Connes and Consani's original author source
\cite{ConnesConsaniHochschild}, Proposition \texttt{laplacian}, describes the finite
spectral quotient by jets and the triangular multiplication action;
the arguments below give the pairing and support maps directly.

For $f\in A$, put
$f^\dagger_\rho(t)=\overline{f_{\iota\rho}(-\bar t)}$.
The functional equation and reality give $g^\dagger=g$ and
$m_{\iota\rho}=m_\rho$, hence this is a well-defined antilinear algebra
involution. Define
\[
 R(f,h)=\sum_{\rho\in Z}\Res_{t=0}
       \frac{f^\dagger_\rho(t)h_\rho(t)}{u_\rho(t)t^{m_\rho}}\,dt,
 \qquad (\Pi_p f)_\rho(t)=p^\rho e^{t\log p}f_\rho(t)\pmod {t^{m_\rho}}.
 \tag{FJ3}\label{fj:fj:raw}
\]
Here $p$ is any positive prime. Multiplication by $p^s$ is a linear
automorphism of $A$, not a unital algebra homomorphism $A\to A$.

\begin{theorem}[Exact coefficient matrix, flags, and duality]
\label{fj:thm:perfect}
Write $u_\rho(t)^{-1}=\sum_{r\ge0}v_{\rho,r}t^r$ and
$f_\rho=\sum_{j<m_\rho}f_{\rho,j}t^j$. Then
\[
 R(f,h)=\sum_{\rho\in Z}\ \sum_{j+k\le m_\rho-1}
 (-1)^jv_{\rho,m_\rho-1-j-k}\,
 \overline{f_{\iota\rho,j}}h_{\rho,k}.
 \tag{FJ4}\label{fj:fj:matrix}
\]
It is perfect and skew-Hermitian. The linear isomorphism
\[
 D_R:\overline A\longrightarrow A^*,\qquad
 D_R(\bar f)(h)=R(f,h)
 \tag{FJ5}\label{fj:fj:dual}
\]
obeys, without a change of the prime factor,
\[
 R(\Pi_p f,\Pi_p h)=pR(f,h),\qquad
 D_R\overline{\Pi_p}=p(\Pi_p^{-1})^*D_R.
 \tag{FJ6}\label{fj:fj:similitude}
\]
For $F^a_\rho=t_\rho^aA_\rho$, $0\le a\le m_\rho$, the annihilator
of $F^b_\rho$ in its reflected partner is $F^{m_\rho-b}_{\iota\rho}$.
The resulting perfect pairing of complementary associated quotients is
\[
 (F^a_{\iota\rho}/F^{a+1}_{\iota\rho})\times
 (F^{m_\rho-1-a}_\rho/F^{m_\rho-a}_\rho)\longrightarrow\C,
 \quad (ct^a,dt^{m_\rho-1-a})\longmapsto
       \frac{(-1)^a\bar c d}{2\lambda_\rho}.
 \tag{FJ7}\label{fj:fj:graded}
\]
On every quotient $F^a_\rho/F^{a+1}_\rho$, the prime eigenvalue is
exactly $p^\rho$, at every jet order $a$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Taking the coefficient of $t^{-1}$ proves \eqref{fj:fj:matrix}.
Changing a representative by a multiple of $g$ adds a holomorphic
integrand, so the formula is well defined. Its antidiagonal coefficient
is $(-1)^jv_{\rho,0}=(-1)^j/(2\lambda_\rho)\ne0$.
Reversing the order of the columns makes this block triangular with
nonzero diagonal. Thus every local block pairs perfectly with its
reflected block and their direct sum is perfect.
For a meromorphic germ $q$, reflection and conjugation send the
coefficient of $t^{-1}$ to minus its conjugate. Applying this to
$q=f^\dagger h/g$ and summing the reflected points proves
$R(h,f)=-\overline{R(f,h)}$.
Furthermore $(p^s)^\dagger=p^{1-s}$, so multiplication of the two
factors inside the residue gives exactly $p$. Replacing $h$ by
$\Pi_p^{-1}h$ gives the second identity in \eqref{fj:fj:similitude}.
Products of elements of orders $a,b$ have order at least $a+b$.
Consequently $F^{m-b}$ annihilates $F^b$. If $f$ has lowest nonzero
order $a<m-b$, choose $h=u_\rho(t)t^{m-1-a}$ times a scalar at the
partner point. It belongs to $F^b$ and its residue against $f$ is
nonzero. This proves the reverse containment. At orders adding to
$m-1$ only $v_{\rho,0}$ occurs, giving \eqref{fj:fj:graded}.
Finally $e^{t\log p}=1+t\log p+\cdots$ proves the action on each quotient.
\end{proof}

The equality $\overline{p^{\iota\rho}}p^\rho=p$ is the eigenvalue
identity in \eqref{fj:fj:graded}. It pairs two reflected points; it does
not force either point individually to be fixed by $\iota$.
All higher jets remain in \eqref{fj:fj:matrix}, with the same eigenvalue
and their nonzero Jordan entries $p^\rho(\log p)^j/j!$.

\begin{theorem}[The Jacobian defines its exact quotient and image]
\label{fj:thm:jacobian}
Let $N=\prod_\rho(t_\rho)$, $E=A/N=\C^Z$, and
$\epsilon:A\to E$ be evaluation. Set $J_g=M_{g'}$ and
\[
 j_g:E\longrightarrow A,\qquad
 (a_\rho)\longmapsto
 (2m_\rho\lambda_\rho a_\rho t_\rho^{m_\rho-1})_\rho.
 \tag{FJ8}\label{fj:fj:jg}
\]
Then $J_g=j_g\epsilon$, $\ker J_g=N$, and
$\im J_g=\prod_\rho\C t_\rho^{m_\rho-1}$, including the whole factor
when $m_\rho=1$. All maps are prime equivariant. The Hermitian form
\[
 T(f,h)=R(f,J_gh)
       =\sum_\rho m_\rho\overline{f(\iota\rho)}h(\rho)
       =\Tr(M_{f^\dagger h}\mid A)
 \tag{FJ9}\label{fj:fj:trace}
\]
has radical exactly $N$, and $T(\Pi_pf,\Pi_ph)=pT(f,h)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Differentiating the unaltered local equation gives
$g'=mu(t)t^{m-1}+u'(t)t^m\equiv2m\lambda t^{m-1}\pmod {t^m}$.
This proves \eqref{fj:fj:jg}, its kernel, and its image. Multiplication
operators commute, and the prime acts by $p^\rho$ both on values and
on the displayed image, proving equivariance. Also
$g'/g=m/t+u'/u$; the second term is holomorphic. Its residue against
$f^\dagger h$ is zero, proving the sum in \eqref{fj:fj:trace} with every
multiplicity. Multiplication by a germ on the $m$-dimensional local
factor has its value on all $m$ diagonal entries, proving the trace
identity. The involution permutes equal multiplicities, proving
Hermitian symmetry. Test against the value vector supported at the
reflected point to detect any nonzero value of $f$; this proves the
radical assertion. The first identity of \eqref{fj:fj:similitude} and
commutation with $J_g$ prove the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
 A \arrow[r,"\epsilon"] \arrow[d,"D_R^r",swap] & E
       \arrow[r,"j_g"] & \im J_g\subset A \arrow[d,"D_R^r"]\\
 (\overline A)^* & & (\overline A)^*
\end{tikzcd}
\caption{The upper composite is $J_g$, with kernel $N$, and its
pullback through the residue duality gives $T$. In this diagram
$D_R^r(h)$ means the equivalent map $\bar f\mapsto R(f,h)$.
The full coefficient matrix is FJ4; the actual factor
$2m_\rho\lambda_\rho$ is FJ8. No Boolean observation occurs.}
\label{fj:fig:jacobian}
\end{figure}

\subsection{The actual tau chart and every retained support label}
Use the original spaces $V,\mathscr B$ of \cite{FJtau}, Section 2:
$V$ consists of even Schwartz functions $\phi$ on $\mathbb R$ with
$\phi(0)=\widehat\phi(0)=0$, with Fourier kernel $e^{-2\pi ix\xi}$;
$\mathscr B$ consists of smooth functions on $\mathbb R_{>0}$ for
which every $x^b(-x\partial_x)^kF$ is bounded, $b\in\mathbb Z$, $k\ge0$.
Set $\Theta\phi(x)=\sum_{n\ne0}\phi(nx)$,
$\mathcal MF(s)=\int_0^\infty F(x)x^s\,dx/x$, and
$J F(x)=x^{-1}F(1/x)$. The four-point chart has
$+<\eta<\sigma$ and $-<\eta<\sigma$ with upper-set topology.
The sheaf has $V,V,\mathscr B,0$ at these points and maps
$\Theta,J\Theta=\Theta\mathcal F$ to $\eta$.
Its cohomology complex and actual prime action are
\[
 C_\tau=[V\oplus V\xrightarrow{d}\mathscr B],\quad
 d(\phi,\psi)=\Theta(\phi-\widehat\psi),\quad
 U_p^0(\phi,\psi)=(U_p\phi,pU_{1/p}\psi),\quad
 U_p^1F(x)=F(x/p).
 \tag{FJ10}\label{fj:fj:tau}
\]
For each nonempty $B\subseteq\{+,-\}$ retain exactly its source
legs, the same $\mathscr B$ at $\eta$, and their original signs.
The empty label has zero complex. Transports insert zero legs and
are the identity at $\eta$. This is the complete original label
lattice $L=\mathcal P(\{+,-\})$, not a chosen branch.

\begin{theorem}[Surjection and dual injection over the whole tau lattice]
\label{fj:thm:tau}
Every nonempty degree-one fibre is $Q=\mathscr B/\Theta V$.
There is a surjective morphism of support diagrams
\[
 q_Z:\{Q_B\}_{B\in L}\longrightarrow\{A_B\}_{B\in L},\quad
 q_Z[F]_\rho=\sum_{j<m_\rho}
       \frac{(\mathcal MF)^{(j)}(\rho)}{j!}t_\rho^j,
 \tag{FJ11}\label{fj:fj:jet}
\]
where $A_B=A$ for $B\ne\varnothing$, $A_\varnothing=0$,
and every nonempty transport is the identity.
It intertwines \eqref{fj:fj:tau} with $\Pi_p$ at every label.
On opposite diagrams, precomposition and \eqref{fj:fj:dual} give an injection
\[
 q_Z^*D_R:\{\overline A_B\}_{L^{\rm op}}\hookrightarrow
        \{\Hom(Q_B,\C(1))\}_{L^{\rm op}},\quad
 \bar f\longmapsto([F]\mapsto R(f,q_Z[F])).
 \tag{FJ12}\label{fj:fj:dual-tau}
\]
Here $\C(1)$ is the actual moment line with prime action $p$, and
the dual action is $\ell\mapsto p\ell U_p^{-1}$.
For the chart's coefficient diagram $\mathcal T$ and right adjoint
$K_\tau(\C(1))$, the target fibre is exactly
\[
 H^{-1}R\Hom(\mathcal T,K_\tau(\C(1)))=\Hom(Q,\C(1)).
\]
The contracted map $\bar f\mapsto([F]\mapsto R(f,J_gq_Z[F]))$
has kernel $\overline N$ in every nonempty source fibre. Labels of
these zero functionals remain distinct.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fourier inversion on even Schwartz functions makes $\mathcal F:V\to V$
an automorphism, so every nonempty leg has image $\Theta V$.
The change of variable $x=py$ gives $\mathcal MU_p=p^s\mathcal M$.
The original Fourier convention gives
$\widehat{U_p\phi}=pU_{1/p}\widehat\phi$, proving $dU_p^0=U_p^1d$.
Every one of these operators is invertible on its retained leg.
For $\Re s>1$, absolute summation gives
$\mathcal M\Theta\phi(s)=2\zeta(s)\int_0^\infty\phi(x)x^{s-1}\,dx$.
The integral is holomorphic for $\Re s>-2$ because evenness and
$\phi(0)=0$ give $\phi(x)=O(x^2)$ at zero. Near any nontrivial
zero the other factors of $g$ are nonzero and holomorphic. Analytic
continuation therefore shows that $\mathcal M\Theta\phi$ is divisible
there by the actual $g$. Thus \eqref{fj:fj:jet} kills the relations.

To prove surjectivity already on compact smooth tests, use $y=\log x$.
Any functional annihilating all their jets would give a finite sum
$\sum_\rho e^{\rho y}P_\rho(y)=0$ on an arbitrary open interval,
where $\deg P_\rho<m_\rho$. To see this, the integral of that sum
against every compact smooth test on the interval is zero; continuity
forces the sum itself to vanish. Analyticity extends the equality to
all $y$. For a fixed $\rho$, apply
$\prod_{\eta\ne\rho}(\partial_y-\eta)^{m_\eta}$.
All other terms vanish, while its operator on $P_\rho$ is
$\prod_{\eta\ne\rho}(\partial_y+\rho-\eta)^{m_\eta}$.
It is invertible on polynomials of degree $<m_\rho$, being triangular
with nonzero constant diagonal. Hence $P_\rho=0$ for every $\rho$.
The jet map has zero annihilator in a finite-dimensional target, so
it is onto. Differentiating $p^s\mathcal MF$ proves its equivariance.

All nonempty support transports are identities on these quotients,
which proves the diagram assertion. Precomposition reverses the arrows.
Surjectivity and the perfect pairing prove the injection; FJ6 proves
its equivariance. For completeness, the projective resolution of the
constant chart diagram is
$0\to P_\eta\to P_+\oplus P_-\to\C\to0$, with maps
$v\mapsto(v,-v)$ and $(u,v)\mapsto u+v$.
It is exact at each of the four points. Applying $\Hom$ gives
\eqref{fj:fj:tau}. Its dual injective complex is
$[I_\eta\C(1)\to I_+\C(1)\oplus I_-\C(1)]$ in degrees $-1,0$,
with maps $(+\mathrm{res},-\mathrm{res})$.
Hom into this complex is the dual of \eqref{fj:fj:tau}; its degree $-1$
kernel is precisely the functionals annihilating $\im d$, namely
$\Hom(Q,\C(1))$. Finally FJ8--9 identify the contracted kernel.
\end{proof}

Reconstruction gives $\mathcal Q=\bigsqcup_{B\in L}Q_B$ and
$\mathcal A=\bigsqcup_{B\in L}A_B$, retaining each label even at zero.
The actual full base
\[
 G_L(\C)=\{(r,1_L):r\in\C\}\cup\{z_B=(0,B):B\in L\}
 \tag{FJ13}
\]
acts by $(r,1_L)(B,v)=(B,rv)$ and
$z_C(B,v)=(B\cap C,0)$. Addition transports to the union label
and adds there. The maps $q_Z,J_g,\epsilon,j_g$ preserve these actions:
each is linear within a fibre and carries its zero to the zero with
the same label. Thus they are maps over the entire lattice base.
Dual reconstruction uses $L^{\rm op}$ and precomposition. Its least
element is the original full-support label and has a nonzero dual
fibre; replacing it by zero would destroy FJ12.
