\subsection{All finite support cochains and their exact dual receiver}
Let $S$ be any nonempty finite $T_0$ support space, ordered so that
opens are lower sets, as in \cite{FJsupport}, formulas
(FL14)--(FL16). Its full constant-coefficient complex is
\[
 C^n(S,A)=\prod_{s_0<\cdots<s_n}A,\qquad
 (dc)_{s_0<\cdots<s_{n+1}}
   =\sum_{a=0}^{n+1}(-1)^ac_{s_0<\cdots<\widehat{s_a}<\cdots<s_{n+1}}.
 \tag{FJ14}\label{fj:fj:support}
\]
There is no degree truncation. Empty products in degrees with no
chains mean the zero vector space. Define $C_n(S,A)$ by the same
finite coordinate sets and the boundary matrix which is the transpose
of this displayed differential. The scalar incidence coefficients
are the original integers $0,1,-1$.

\begin{theorem}[Perfect support cohomology--homology duality]
\label{fj:thm:support}
The prime acts on every coefficient in FJ14 by $\Pi_p$ and commutes
with every differential and boundary. The pairing
\[
 \mathcal R_n(a,c)=\sum_{s_0<\cdots<s_n}R(a_{s_0<\cdots<s_n},
                                           c_{s_0<\cdots<s_n})
 \tag{FJ15}\label{fj:fj:chain-pair}
\]
descends to a perfect pairing
$H_n(S,A)\times H^n(S,A)\to\C$ and obeys the exact prime
similitude $\mathcal R_n(\Pi_pa,\Pi_pc)=p\mathcal R_n(a,c)$.
There are canonical isomorphisms
\[
 H^n(S,A)=H^n(S,\C)\otimes_\C A,\qquad
 H_n(S,A)=H_n(S,\C)\otimes_\C A.
 \tag{FJ16}\label{fj:fj:kunneth}
\]
The Jacobian acts as $1\otimes J_g$. Its kernel and image on either
side are, respectively, $H^{n}(S,\C)\otimes N$ and
$H^{n}(S,\C)\otimes\im J_g$, with $H_n$ in the homological formula.
Consequently the contraction of FJ15 by $J_g$ has exactly these
radicals and retains the factor $m_\rho$ on its value quotient.

For an order-preserving map $f:S'\to S$, use
\[
 (f^*c)_{t_0<\cdots<t_n}=
 \begin{cases}
 c_{f(t_0)<\cdots<f(t_n)},&\text{all image points distinct},\\
 0,&\text{otherwise},
 \end{cases}
 \tag{FJ17}
\]
and its transpose chain map $f_*$. They commute with all prime and
Jacobian maps, and
$\mathcal R_n(f_*a,c)=\mathcal R_n(a,f^*c)$ on chains and homology.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Expanding $d^2$ pairs every two deleted vertices in its two possible
orders with opposite signs; thus it is zero. The chain assertion is
its transpose. Scalar incidence matrices commute with coefficient
multiplication. Transposition and the real signs give
$\mathcal R_n(a,db)=\mathcal R_{n-1}(\partial a,b)$.
It follows that cycles pair trivially with boundaries, on either side.
Each coordinate pairing is perfect by Theorem~\ref{fj:thm:perfect}.
In a finite-dimensional perfect pairing, the annihilator of a kernel
is the image of the adjoint matrix: containment follows by evaluation,
and equality follows because both dimensions equal the rank of that
matrix. Apply this once to each of the two adjacent differentials.
The induced pairing on the two homology quotients is perfect.

Every chain module in FJ14 is its scalar chain module tensored with
$A$. Choosing any vector-space complements to its kernels and images
proves that tensoring with $A$ preserves both kernels and images.
The resulting isomorphisms FJ16 are canonical because their maps send
$[c]\otimes a$ to $[c\otimes a]$ independently of those complements.
Apply the same exactness to FJ8 and use perfectness of the homology
pairing; this proves all kernel, image, and radical assertions.
Coefficientwise FJ6 gives the prime factor $p$.

For FJ17, if there is an adjacent repetition in the image chain,
the two terms deleting its equal members cancel and every other term
still has a repetition. Without a repetition the ordinary face
formula gives $df^*=f^*d$. Transposition gives the chain identity.
The defining finite sums give the adjointness, and coefficientwise
operators commute with these scalar matrices. All identities therefore
descend to the stated groups.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The exact augmented support quotient]
\label{fj:cor:augmented}
Put $K^0=A$, $K^n=C^{n-1}(S,A)$ for $n\ge1$, with
$d^0a=(a)_{s\in S}$ and $d^n=d_C^{n-1}$. If $S$ has $c$ connected
components, then
\[
 H^0(K)=0,\quad H^1(K)=A^c/\Delta A,
 \quad H^n(K)=H^{n-1}(S,\C)\otimes A\quad(n\ge2).
 \tag{FJ18}\label{fj:fj:augmented}
\]
The first nonzero group is paired perfectly with
$\{(a_1,\ldots,a_c)\in A^c:\sum_i a_i=0\}$ by
$\sum_iR(a_i,b_i)$. This pairing, its Jacobian contraction, every
support pullback, and the prime similitude follow from FJ15 without
discarding a support component.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The diagonal is injective because $S\ne\varnothing$.
Degree-zero cocycles of the support complex are exactly functions
constant on each connected component, proving FJ18. Changing a
representative $b$ by $\Delta h$ changes the pairing by
$R(\sum_i a_i,h)=0$. Conversely this annihilator of $\Delta A$
is exactly the stated kernel by perfectness of $R$. The quotient
pairing is therefore perfect. The remaining assertions are the
cochain identities already proved, including the augmentation.
\end{proof}

For any declared support-trajectory lattice of the incidence matrices,
restrict FJ14 to its upward-closed coordinate trajectories. Extension
by zero gives the original cochain maps: closure ensures that no
nonzero matrix entry leaves the next support. Tensoring each such
complex with $A$, as in FJ16, preserves its entire cohomology diagram.
Its dual diagram reverses arrows by matrix transpose. In particular
an inclusion on cochains need not be injective on cohomology; its
dual map is the transpose of the actual induced map, with the full
kernel preserved. This is the precise receiver for the full support
construction of \cite{FJsupport}, not a replacement by Boolean stalks.

\subsection{The spaces defined by the positivity and descent defects}
\begin{theorem}[Every positive semidefinite prime-similitude form]
\label{fj:thm:psd}
The Hermitian positive semidefinite forms $B$ on the entire $A_Z$
satisfying $B(\Pi_pf,\Pi_ph)=pB(f,h)$ for both $p=2,3$ are exactly
\[
 B(f,h)=\sum_{\substack{\rho\in Z\\\Re\rho=1/2}}
             b_\rho\overline{f(\rho)}h(\rho),\qquad b_\rho\ge0.
 \tag{FJ19}\label{fj:fj:psd}
\]
Thus their common forced radical is the prime-invariant ideal
\[
 \mathfrak D_Z=
 \left(\prod_{\Re\rho\ne1/2}A_\rho\right)
 \oplus\left(\prod_{\Re\rho=1/2}(t_\rho)\right),
 \qquad A_Z/\mathfrak D_Z=\C^{Z\cap(1/2+i\mathbb R)}.
 \tag{FJ20}\label{fj:fj:defect}
\]
The quotient map is a prime-equivariant algebra homomorphism and is
the universal quotient through which all forms FJ19 factor. A positive
definite prime-similitude form on $A_Z$ exists exactly when every point
of $Z$ lies on the critical line and every $m_\rho=1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $N_B$ be the radical of a positive semidefinite Hermitian form.
Cauchy--Schwarz follows by evaluating $B(v+zw,v+zw)\ge0$ for complex
$z$, and proves that $B(v,v)=0$ is equivalent to $v\in N_B$.
The two prime similitudes and invertibility of $\Pi_p$ make $N_B$
invariant under $\Pi_p$ and its inverse. On $A/N_B$ each
$p^{-1/2}\Pi_p$ is unitary. A finite-dimensional unitary operator is
diagonalizable: an eigenvector exists over $\C$, its orthogonal
complement is invariant under the operator and its inverse, and
induction produces an orthonormal eigenbasis. Its eigenvalues have
modulus one by its norm identity.

The local image of $A_\rho$ in this quotient is annihilated by
$(\Pi_2-2^\rho)^{m_\rho}$. If $\Re\rho\ne1/2$, the eigenvalue
$2^\rho$ has the wrong modulus, so the image is zero. On a critical
factor, diagonalizability forces $\Pi_2-2^\rho$ to annihilate the
image. But
$\Pi_2-2^\rho=2^\rho t_\rho a(t_\rho)$, with
$a(0)=\log2\ne0$, so its image ideal is exactly $(t_\rho)$.
Thus every nonconstant jet in a critical factor also maps to zero.

For remaining value vectors at $\rho,\eta$, equivariance gives
$(\overline{p^\rho}p^\eta-p)B(e_\rho,e_\eta)=0$ for $p=2,3$.
At critical points a nonzero cross term requires
$p^\rho=p^\eta$ for both primes. Then
$(\rho-\eta)\log2=2\pi ik$ and
$(\rho-\eta)\log3=2\pi il$. A zero integer forces $\rho=\eta$;
otherwise division gives a rational relation between $\log2$ and
$\log3$, contradicting unique factorization after exponentiation.
Therefore distinct critical values are orthogonal. Their diagonal
weights are nonnegative, proving FJ19. Conversely that formula gives
a positive semidefinite form and its prime similitudes directly.
Choosing every weight strictly positive proves that the intersection
of all their radicals is exactly FJ20. The final claim follows because
FJ20 is zero exactly in the stated case.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The full support multiplicity spaces]
For a finite-dimensional complex vector space $W$, all positive
semidefinite Hermitian forms on $W\otimes A$ compatible with
$1\otimes\Pi_2$ and $1\otimes\Pi_3$ are exactly
\[
 B(v,w)=\sum_{\Re\rho=1/2}B_\rho(v(\rho),w(\rho)),
\]
where each $B_\rho$ is an arbitrary positive semidefinite Hermitian
form on $W$. Their common forced radical is $W\otimes\mathfrak D_Z$.
This applies to each of $W=H^n(S,\C)$,
$W=H_n(S,\C)$ and $W=\C^c/\Delta\C$ in FJ16--18.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The quotient-by-radical argument of Theorem~\ref{fj:thm:psd} annihilates
each off-line factor and each $W\otimes(t_\rho)$ in a critical factor.
Distinct critical eigenvalue pairs are orthogonal by the same two-prime
calculation. Within a fixed eigenvalue pair the prime action is scalar
on $W$, so it imposes no further condition on the positive semidefinite
form on that space. Conversely any displayed family gives both prime
similitudes. Choosing a positive definite form on every nonzero $W$
proves the common-radical assertion.
\end{proof}

For the original $T$, each critical point contributes the weight
$m_\rho$. A reflected nonfixed pair contributes
$m\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$ on
its values and has a negative vector $(1,-1)$. Thus the exact
similitude FJ6 and FJ9 exists before any positivity. FJ19 specifies
all possible positive replacements and their precise lost data; it
does not establish that the actual $T$ belongs to that set for all
zeros. In particular a finite positive restriction of the original
trace proves no universal positivity assertion.

There is also an exact obstruction to obtaining a scalar-valued
pairing from support labels alone. For a diagram $M_B$ on a finite
join lattice with top $1$, let $\mathcal M=\bigsqcup_BM_B$ with
addition by transport to joins. Its universal map to an additive
group is
\[
 \varepsilon_{\rm top}:\mathcal M\to M_1,
       \quad(B,v)\longmapsto h_{B1}v.
 \tag{FJ21}\label{fj:fj:top}
\]
Indeed every label zero $(B,0)$ is additively idempotent and must
map to zero in a group, while
$(B,v)+(1,0)=(1,h_{B1}v)$. This proves both factorization and its
uniqueness. Consequently any complex-valued pairing additive in each
entry factors through FJ21 in both entries. The exact object killed
in fibre $B$ is $\ker h_{B1}$; retaining its diagram is necessary
to preserve all support cohomology. The opposite dual diagram used
in FJ12 and FJ15 does preserve it and supplies the proved connecting
maps. At the still coarser coefficient-support observation of
\cite{FJdivisor}, vectors $(1,1)$ and $(1,-1)$ on a reflected pair
have identical support but opposite values $2m,-2m$ under $T$.
This computes a failure of factorization, while FJ11--18 give the
full coefficient and cohomological maps which do exist.

\subsection{The prime action on every declared jet support}
Fix an actual factor $A_\rho$ of multiplicity $m$ and its unaltered
coordinate basis $1,t_\rho,\ldots,t_\rho^{m-1}$. A declared support is
a subset $B\subseteq\{0,\ldots,m-1\}$. Its carrier element $(B,x)$
has $x\in\C^B$, and zero coordinates in $B$ remain supported zeros.
Outside $B$ there is external absence. Put
$\uparrow B=\{k:\text{some }j\in B\text{ has }j\le k\}$.

\begin{theorem}[Full prime support lift and its cancellation cells]
\label{fj:thm:prime-support}
The actual prime matrix has entries
\[
 (P_{p,\rho})_{kj}=
 \begin{cases}p^\rho(\log p)^{k-j}/(k-j)!,&k\ge j,\\0,&k<j.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{FJ22}\label{fj:fj:prime-matrix}
\]
Every entry in its lower triangle is nonzero. Its declared-support
lift to $G(\C)^m$ is the exact semimodule map
\[
 \widetilde\Pi_{p,\rho}(B,x)
   =(\uparrow B,\ (P_{p,\rho}x)|_{\uparrow B}).
 \tag{FJ23}\label{fj:fj:supported-prime}
\]
For positive integers $a,b>1$ these lifts satisfy
$\widetilde\Pi_a\widetilde\Pi_b=\widetilde\Pi_{ab}$.
For the action with parameter $1/p$, the composite with FJ23 is
$(B,x)\mapsto(\uparrow B,x^{\rm ext})$, where $x^{\rm ext}$ adds
supported zeros. It is the identity precisely on the subdiagram of
upward-closed supports, whose coefficient fibres are the full ideals
$F^a_\rho=t_\rho^aA_\rho$.

There is an exact linear isomorphism to the source's derivative stalk
\[
 s_\rho:A_\rho\longrightarrow E_\rho=t_\rho\C[t_\rho]/(t_\rho^{m+1}),
       \quad f\longmapsto t_\rho f,
 \tag{FJ24}
\]
intertwining multiplication by $p^\rho e^{t_\rho\log p}$ on both
spaces. Its composition with the original pointed map $\sigma_\rho$
records the actual nonzero coefficients, with index shifted by one.
For $m\ge2$ no map on these numerical support values alone
intertwines this prime action for all $f\in A_\rho$.

The exact replacement is the finite family of coefficient spaces
\[
 \mathcal C_{p;B,C}=\left\{x\in\C^m:
 \begin{array}{ll}
 x_j\ne0\ \Longleftrightarrow\ j\in B,\\
 \displaystyle p^\rho\sum_{j=0}^k
     \frac{(\log p)^{k-j}}{(k-j)!}x_j\ne0
       \ \Longleftrightarrow\ k\in C
 \end{array}\right\}.
 \tag{FJ25}\label{fj:fj:cells}
\]
They are disjoint locally closed algebraic subsets, some possibly
empty, which partition $A_\rho$. Multiplication by $P_{p,\rho}$
gives the explicit isomorphism
$\mathcal C_{p;B,C}\to\mathcal C_{1/p;C,B}$ with inverse
$P_{1/p,\rho}$. Thus the failed support descent determines a
specified family of coefficient spaces and exact maps.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Expanding the original exponential gives FJ22. Matrix multiplication
in $G(\C)$ gives support $\uparrow B$ even if an amplitude cancels;
this proves FJ23 including all its supported zeros. The exponential
identity gives $P_aP_b=P_{ab}$, with every prime factor and logarithm
unchanged. Since $\uparrow\uparrow B=\uparrow B$, the supports of
the two lifted sides also agree for $a,b>1$. The inverse-parameter
matrix is lower triangular with every lower entry nonzero as well.
Its ring composite is the identity matrix, but its declared composite
retains every lower triangular path. Thus its value on $(B,x)$ has
exactly the support and amplitudes stated after FJ23. Equality with
$(B,x)$ is precisely $B=\uparrow B$. Such subsets are the terminal
segments $\{a,\ldots,m-1\}$ and the empty set, with coefficient
spaces exactly $F^a_\rho$. Their restriction maps are inclusions;
FJ7 is the full reflected duality of this flag diagram.

The map $s_\rho$ is well defined and its inverse divides its polynomial
representative by $t_\rho$, modulo $t_\rho^m$. Commutation with
coefficient multiplication proves its equivariance. For failure of
the numerical support action, put $L=\log p$ and compare
$f=1+Lt_\rho$ and $h=1-Lt_\rho$, both having the same nonzero
coefficient indices $\{0,1\}$. The coefficient of $t_\rho$ in their
prime images is respectively $2p^\rho L\ne0$ and zero. This remains
valid for every $m\ge2$, regardless of later coefficients.
Therefore no function of the input numerical support can give the
output numerical support for both inputs.
Finally FJ25 consists of linear equations together with inequalities
that finitely many linear polynomials are nonzero, proving local
closedness. Every $x$ has one input and one output support, proving
the partition. Invertibility of $P_{p,\rho}$ and its explicit inverse
give both the asserted bijection and its regular inverse.
\end{proof}

The closure $B\mapsto\uparrow B$ preserves joins and bottom; it
does not preserve every meet. For $m\ge2$, $B=\{0\}$ and
$C=\{1\}$ have empty intersection, whereas their upper closures
intersect. The exact general comparison is
$\uparrow(B\cap C)\subseteq\uparrow B\cap\uparrow C$.
Consequently the unrestricted support lift is a map of the original
$G(\C)$ semimodules with this support transport; it is not silently
a scalar-base automorphism of $G_{\mathcal P(\{0,\ldots,m-1\})}(\C)$.
On the complete terminal-flag diagram the support map is the identity,
the entire prime action is invertible, and every full jet is still
present in its largest coefficient fibre. The products of these
constructions over the actual $\rho\in Z$ retain every multiplicity,
reflection partner, and support coordinate.


\section{The finite projective receiver of the full arithmetic jets}
\label{sec:projective-jet-receiver}

The new full-support reconstruction supplied with this calculation uses
finite projective modules to receive ordinary matrices. We prove that
construction here and apply it to the complete jet pairing above.
The scalar object is the original $S=G_L(\mathbb C)$ of version 11
\cite{support}; $L$ is any nontrivial bounded distributive lattice.
No choice of Boolean branch or replacement of an amplitude zero by
absence occurs in these maps.

For positive integers $n$ let $P_n$ have entries $1^\bullet$ on
the diagonal and $e$ off the diagonal. The free identity has $\tau$
off the diagonal. Multiplication in the original semiring gives
\[
 P_n^2=P_n,\qquad
 \mathcal V_n:=P_nS^n
 =\{(a,x):a=1_L,\ x\in\mathbb C^n\}
       \sqcup\{(a,0):a<1_L\}.
 \tag{JC1}
\]
The displayed coordinates identify $(a,0)$ with the constant vector
$z_a$, and $(1_L,x)$ with its fully supported coordinate vector.
To verify the image exactly, if one input coordinate has top support,
each row acquires top support through multiplication by $1^\bullet$
or $e$, and its amplitude is the original coordinate amplitude.
If all supports are lower, every entry in the output has the common
join of those supports and zero amplitude; the join is allowed to
be top. Conversely each displayed vector is fixed by $P_n$.
This proves both the image and idempotence and realizes $\mathcal V_n$
as a retract of the free module, hence as finite projective.

For a matrix $A\in M_{m,n}(\mathbb C)$ let $\widehat A$ have
all entries supported, including every zero. Then
\[
 P_m\widehat A P_n=\widehat A,\qquad
 \widehat A\widehat B=\widehat{AB},\qquad
 \widehat {I_n}=P_n,\qquad \widehat 0=eP_n.
 \tag{JC2}
\]
Each matrix entry is a nonempty sum of supported products, so its
amplitude is the ordinary matrix product and its support is top even
when the sum cancels. This proves every formula in (JC2).
Consequently all invertible ordinary matrices become automorphisms
of these actual projective modules, with inverse $\widehat{A^{-1}}$.

More precisely, for arbitrary $X\in M_{m,n}(S)$, the entry supports
of $P_mXP_n$ are all the join of the supports of every entry of $X$;
its amplitude matrix is the original amplitude matrix. Therefore
\[
 P_mM_{m,n}(S)P_n
 =\{\widehat A:A\in M_{m,n}(\mathbb C)\}
       \sqcup\{z_a\mathbf1_{m,n}:a<1_L\}.
 \tag{JC3}
\]
Every matrix in the displayed set occurs. In the square case this
is the exact semiring $G_L(M_n(\mathbb C))$, with its possibly
noncommutative amplitude multiplication and identity $P_n$.
The support-preserving maps in (JC3) are precisely the fully
supported ones: they act by $(1,x)\mapsto(1,Ax)$ and fix every
lower support. A lower constant matrix instead meets all input
supports with $a$ and cannot preserve top. Composition is exactly
ordinary matrix composition. The relative zero map is $\widehat0$.
The relative zero object is $eS\simeq L$, with zero-dimensional
arithmetic fibre; the unique support-preserving arrows to and from
it justify that description. This includes dimension zero without
identifying the object with the absent semimodule.

With lexicographic ordering of tensor coordinates,
$P_n\otimes P_m=P_{nm}$: a diagonal entry is $1^\bullet$ and
every other entry has one or two factors $e$, whose product is $e$.
The usual explicit tensor basis maps of finite free semimodules
identify $\mathcal V_n\otimes_S\mathcal V_m$ with the image of
this projector. Thus the projective realization also retains every
ordinary tensor operation.

For original positive Hermitian matrices $G,H$, give $\mathcal V_n$
the pairing which equals $(x^*Gy)^\bullet$ on the top fibre and
$z_{a\wedge b}$ whenever one input is in lower support.
Sesquilinearity follows by checking whether a join reaches top:
lower inputs have zero amplitude, and their joins and meets follow
distributivity in $L$; on the top fibre it is ordinary
sesquilinearity. Hence the actual adjoint identity is
\[
 \widehat A^{\dagger_{G,H}}=\widehat{G^{-1}A^*H}.
 \tag{JC4}
\]
Testing both top inputs proves the amplitudes, and a lower input
on either side gives the same meet of supports. No new metric
is substituted for the source or target metric.

The trace on a finite projective retract is computed by inclusion,
the endomorphism, and the retraction in its free ambient module.
It is independent of the retract presentation: if maps $U,V$ give
inverse projective identifications, the equality
$\operatorname{tr}(XY)=\operatorname{tr}(YX)$ follows by writing
both finite double sums over the commutative scalar semiring and
interchanging their indices. Applying that identity to the retract
maps proves invariance. For (JC2) it gives
\[
 \operatorname{tr}_S(\widehat A)
       =(\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathbb C}A)^\bullet,
 \quad
 p\operatorname{tr}_S(\widehat A)=\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathbb C}A,
 \quad
 \chi\operatorname{tr}_S(\widehat A)=1_L.
 \tag{JC5}
\]
The last equality holds even if the amplitude trace is zero.
After scalar extension by $p:S\to\mathbb C$, the projector is
$I_n$. After extension by localization $\chi:S\to L=S[e^{-1}]$,
it is the all-one matrix, whose image is the common-coordinate
copy of $L$; every $\widehat A$ becomes the identity on this image.
These follow by tensoring the two retract arrows, so identify the
actual base changes, including their trace maps.

Now take the full packet $A_Z$ of (FJ1)--(FJ3), of dimension
$d=\sum_\rho m_\rho$, with every local unit retained. Choose the
ordered monomial jet coordinates already used there. Its perfect
residue pairing has an invertible matrix $D_R$. Equation (JC2)
therefore transports its full dual isomorphism to the projective
corner, with inverse $\widehat{D_R^{-1}}$. The transported pairing
has the same full amplitude $R$ and value $z_{a\wedge b}$ on
lower supports. The prime matrix and the exact duality give
\[
 \widehat{D_R}\,\widehat{\overline{\Pi_p}}
  =\widehat{p(\Pi_p^{-1})^*}\,\widehat{D_R}.
 \tag{JC6}
\]
This is the lifted equality of the original matrices in (FJ4)--(FJ7),
proved by (JC2), and retains every higher jet and the original factor
$p$. In particular the inverse prime matrix is an actual projective
automorphism without assuming it is a free split automorphism.

The Jacobian map $J_g$ from (FJ8)--(FJ10) has ordinary kernel the
nilradical and image the socle. Its supported-zero kernel on the
top fibre is precisely that same kernel, and every lower support
is retained with zero amplitude. Thus the exact support-preserving
kernel and quotient are
\[
 \mathcal V(\operatorname{nil}A_Z)\hookrightarrow\mathcal V(A_Z)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal V(A_Z/\operatorname{nil}A_Z),
 \qquad (a,x)\longmapsto(a,[x]).
 \tag{JC7}
\]
Here $\mathcal V(W)$ is (JC1) with top fibre $W$; for $W=0$
it is $L$. The last map has exactly the displayed fibrewise kernel,
is onto on every fibre, and preserves all support transitions.
The full residue pairing is perfect before this quotient; the
ordinary arithmetic trace pairing factors through it by (FJ10).
After localization every object and map in (JC7) is $L$ with the
identity, including its fibrewise zero object. This describes the
entire degeneration rather than assigning that identity a complex
trace of one. The amplitude and support trace receivers are exactly
(JC5), with the actual connecting scalar maps stated there.

\section{The full-addition base product and its surviving support geometry}
\label{sec:full-support-base-product}

The supplied arithmetic-specificity calculation computes the self-product
of the scalar globalization. Here we retain the entire finite lattice
of the original version 11 definition \cite{support}. The result specifies
which coordinates agree in the product, which remain independent, and
the new relative cohomology they support. The blueprint conventions are
those of Lorscheid \cite{lorscheid}, original author TeX
\path{blueprints-v2.tex}, axioms (A1)--(A9),
\texttt{lemma\_proper}, and Section 1.12's tensor construction.
We use the term properization for its quotient by equality relations
between individual elements. Semiring completion also adds the values
of formal sums; the two operations below have different explicit images.

\subsection{The integer coordinate in every common target}

Put $S=G(\mathbb Z)$ and denote its supported integers by $j(n)$,
with $j(0)=e$ and its additive identity $\tau$. For maps $f,g$ from
the full-addition blueprint of $S$ to a common proper blueprint, set
$u=f(j(-1))$ and $v=g(j(-1))$. Both satisfy the original relations
\[
 1+u+1\equiv1,\qquad u+1+u\equiv u,
 \qquad 1+v+1\equiv1,\qquad v+1+v\equiv v.
 \tag{BP1}
\]
In the commutative monoid of formal sums modulo preaddition,
these regular inverses of $1$ are unique. Indeed
\[
 u\equiv u+1+v+1+u\equiv u+1+v,
 \qquad v\equiv v+1+u+1+v\equiv u+1+v.
 \tag{BP2}
\]
The first equality in each line replaces $1$ by $1+v+1$ or
$1+u+1$ inside the corresponding regular-inverse identity;
the second uses $u+1+u\equiv u$ or $v+1+v\equiv v$.
Properness implies $u=v$. Then $f(e)\equiv1+u\equiv g(e)$,
so these also agree. Repeated addition determines every positive
integer from $1$ and every negative integer from $u$; both maps send
$\tau$ to the unique zero. Thus $f=g$ on the entire scalar $S$.
This proof uses the regular inverse, not an additive inverse of $1$
with respect to $\tau$.

Consequently, for any specified blueprint base $B\to S$,
\[
 (B_S\otimes_B B_S)^{\rm prop}\simeq B_S,
 \qquad S\otimes_{\mathbb N}S\simeq S.
 \tag{BP3}
\]
For either assertion, pairs of agreeing maps into the relevant common
target are exactly single maps, by (BP2). The inverse arrows are
the inclusion $a\mapsto a\otimes1$ and multiplication
$a\otimes b\mapsto ab$. Their composites are identities by that
universal property. This proves the scalar calculation in the arrival.
In particular $e\otimes1=1\otimes e$; the actual prime $(e)$ remains
present and does not become two different arithmetic coordinates.

\subsection{The full lattice product in commutative semirings}

Let $P,Q$ be nonempty finite posets, with their topology of lower
sets. Put $L=\operatorname{Down}(P)$, $M=\operatorname{Down}(Q)$,
and $T=\operatorname{Down}(P\times Q)$. The exact coproduct is
\[
 G_L(\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb N}G_M(\mathbb Z)
       \simeq G_T(\mathbb Z).
 \tag{BP4}
\]
The structure maps send every supported integer to the same integer
and send the zero-amplitude supports by
\[
 A\longmapsto A\times Q,\qquad B\longmapsto P\times B.
 \tag{BP5}
\]
Their product on zero-amplitude elements has support $A\times B$.
All formulas preserve both $\tau$ and supported $e$ in their exact
roles.

Here is a proof of the whole universal property. Given semiring maps
$f:G_L(\mathbb Z)\to C$ and $g:G_M(\mathbb Z)\to C$, (BP2)
makes their supported integer maps agree. Write $E$ for their common
value of $e$, and $a_A=f(0,A)$, $b_B=g(0,B)$. Each is additively
and multiplicatively idempotent, is absorbed by $E$ under addition,
and has $E$ as a multiplicative identity. The finite sums of their
products form a bounded distributive lattice with join given by
addition, meet by multiplication, bottom $0_C$ and top $E$.
For detail, sums of commuting multiplicative idempotents which are
also additively idempotent remain idempotent under either operation.
Cross absorption holds since $b_B+E=E$ implies
$a_Ab_B+a_AE=a_AE=a_A$. Distributivity and this identity prove both
absorption laws on the generated set. The two original lattice maps
therefore take values in this common bounded lattice.

The universal lattice map from $T$ has the explicit value
\[
 F(U)=\bigvee_{(p,q)\in U} a_{\downarrow p}\,b_{\downarrow q}.
 \tag{BP6}
\]
It preserves unions immediately. For intersections, distribute the
two joins and use
$\downarrow p\cap\downarrow p'
 =\bigcup_{r\le p,p'}\downarrow r$, and the identical formula
in $Q$. The resulting terms are exactly those indexed by $U\cap U'$.
It preserves bottom and top. The same expansion proves
$F(A\times Q)=a_A$ and $F(P\times B)=b_B$. Every lower set is the
union of its principal rectangles, so (BP6) is the unique such map.

Extend $F$ to $G_T(\mathbb Z)$ by the common supported integer map.
A supported integer absorbs each generator under addition and
acts identically on it by multiplication; these identities hold
first in its original factor and then for products and joins.
They also hold for a negative supported integer, because both factors
have the identical map $j(-1)$ proved in (BP2). Thus the extension
preserves the mixed operations. It agrees at $e$, so is well-defined.
It is the required unique semiring map. This proves (BP4).

For arbitrary finite nontrivial bounded distributive lattices this
description includes every case, rather than a chosen subfamily.
To see this directly, write $P$ for the nonzero join-irreducible
elements of $L$, with inherited order. Every element is the join
of the join-irreducibles below it, by induction in the finite order.
Distributivity implies a join-irreducible below a finite join lies
below one summand: for two summands intersect it with the join and
use its defining irreducibility, then induct. Hence
$a\mapsto\{p:p\le a\}$ is a lattice isomorphism
$L\simeq\operatorname{Down}(P)$, with inverse the join.
The same proof applies to $M$.

\subsection{Proper blueprint product, semiring completion, and their exact map}

Use the base blueprint with zero $\mathbb F_1^{\rm mon}=\{0,1\}$,
with only its zero relation, and let $\mathcal B$ be the raw
blueprint tensor of the two full-addition factors. In the description
of (BP4), its properization is the subblueprint with underlying set
\[
 \mathcal A_{P,Q}
 =\{(n,P\times Q):n\in\mathbb Z\}
       \ \cup\ \{(0,A\times B):A\in L,\ B\in M\},
 \tag{BP7}
\]
and preaddition the equality of its finite sums in $G_T(\mathbb Z)$.
In particular the exact connecting maps are
\[
 \mathcal B\longrightarrow\mathcal B^{\rm prop}
   \simeq\mathcal A_{P,Q}\hookrightarrow B_{G_T(\mathbb Z)},
 \qquad (\mathcal B^{\rm prop})_{\mathbb N}=G_T(\mathbb Z).
 \tag{BP8}
\]

Indeed the raw underlying elements are pure tensors; the finite sums
modulo their preaddition form its universal semiring completion.
Maps from that completion to $C$ are the two original semiring maps,
so (BP4) identifies this completion exactly. A pure tensor has the
value $(nm,P\times Q)$ when both amplitudes are nonzero, and otherwise
has amplitude zero and rectangular support as in (BP7). All elements
of (BP7) occur. Equality of images of two raw elements in the semiring
completion is exactly their preaddition equivalence, by its definition
as the quotient of formal sums. Properization consequently has
precisely (BP7)'s elements and induced preaddition. Adding all their
finite sums gives all lower sets by principal rectangles, proving
the last assertion of (BP8). Multiplication of rectangles closes
(BP7), so its indicated subblueprint is defined.

This identifies a material extra operation. If $P=Q$ consists of
two incomparable points, the union
$\{(p_1,p_1),(p_2,p_2)\}$ is a lower set but is not a rectangle.
Its zero-amplitude value is a sum in the semiring completion, while
it is absent from the underlying set (BP7). This is an explicit
example and exact embedding, with no identification of properization
with adding all sums. For scalar one-point $P,Q$, every lower set
is a rectangle and (BP8) reduces to (BP3).

\subsection{The changed prime spectrum and the diagonal}

The full spectrum theorem already proved in this manuscript gives
\[
 \operatorname{Spec}G_T(\mathbb Z)
     =(P\times Q)\mathbin{\blacktriangleright}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z.
 \tag{BP9}
\]
For completeness the lattice prime associated to $(p,q)$ is
$\{U:(p,q)\notin U\}$: a union avoids the point exactly when
both sets do, and an intersection avoids it exactly when one does.
Every finite lattice prime has this form, since its complement is
a prime filter, whose smallest element is a principal lower set;
the join-irreducibility proof following (BP6) identifies its generator.
The general support/arithmetic classification then proves (BP9).
The two projection maps on spectra are
\[
 (p,q)\mapsto p,\quad (p,q)\mapsto q;
 \qquad \mathfrak p\mapsto\mathfrak p
       \quad(\mathfrak p\in\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z).
 \tag{BP10}
\]
Testing inverse images against (BP5) proves these assertions point
by point. There are no support/arithmetic mixed points: the two
pulled-back supported zeros are the same element $e$, so any prime
contains both or neither. Every integer and its original prime
vanishing set is retained on the single arithmetic stratum.

For $P=Q$, multiplication of the two equal factors induces the
surjective map
\[
 G_{\operatorname{Down}(P\times P)}(\mathbb Z)
       \longrightarrow G_{\operatorname{Down}(P)}(\mathbb Z),
 \qquad (0,U)\mapsto(0,\{p:(p,p)\in U\}),
 \quad j(n)\mapsto j(n).
 \tag{BP11}
\]
It preserves joins and meets because it is inverse image under
the diagonal, so is a semiring map; it is surjective using $A\times P$.
Its spectrum map is the diagonal on the full support and the identity
on the arithmetic stratum. Its exact congruence identifies two null
supports precisely when their diagonal intersections agree, and
identifies no distinct nonzero amplitudes. This gives the complete
defect object and comparison map of the self-product calculation.

\subsection{An explicit product with surviving relative cohomology}

Take $P$ to be the six-element face poset of the boundary of an
oriented triangle: three vertices and its three edges, ordered by
incidence. This is the earlier (FL) support example. The poset
$P\times P$ is the face poset of the product cell structure on
the product of the two boundary triangles. Its order complex is
the barycentric subdivision of that product. An explicit realization
sends a pair of faces to the product of their barycentres and extends
affinely on each chain. On each product face, the chains ending in
that face form the cone from its barycentre over the subdivided
boundary. Induction over dimensions $0,1,2$ shows these cones tile
each convex product face with disjoint interiors and agree on common
boundaries. Thus this realization is a homeomorphism, including every
one of the $36$ original support points.

Here is also a direct integral calculation. The triangle boundary
has cellular boundary $\partial e_i=v_{i+1}-v_i$ (indices modulo
three). Choose $e_0,e_1$ as a spanning tree. Their boundaries and
$v_0$ form an integral basis of the vertex group; the edge basis
$e_0,e_1,z=e_0+e_1+e_2$ splits the chain complex into two identity
complexes and a copy of $\mathbb Z$ in each degree $0,1$.
The product cells have boundary
$\partial(a\times b)=\partial a\times b+(-1)^{\deg a}a\times\partial b$,
as follows by orienting each rectangular face by its two edge
coordinates. Tensoring the displayed splitting leaves its two
identity factors contractible and gives
\[
 H^j(P\times P,\mathbb Q)=
 \begin{cases}\mathbb Q&j=0,2,\\\mathbb Q^2&j=1,\\0&j>2.\end{cases}
 \tag{BP12}
\]
Write $\alpha_1,\alpha_2$ for the edge-circuit cohomology classes
pulled back from the factors, each evaluating to one on $z$.
Their product evaluates to one on the oriented product $z\times z$.
The diagonal pulls both to $\alpha$ and their degree-two product to
zero, since the one-dimensional boundary has no degree-two cochains.

Apply the actual relative complex (FL14),
$[\mathbb Z\to C^\bullet(P\times P,\mathbb Q)]$, with the
integer term in degree zero and the support terms shifted by one.
The constants map is injective, and (BP12) gives
\[
 H_X^1=\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z,\qquad
 H_X^2=\mathbb Q\alpha_1\oplus\mathbb Q\alpha_2,\qquad
 H_X^3=\mathbb Q(\alpha_1\alpha_2),\qquad H_X^{j>3}=0.
 \tag{BP13}
\]
The morphism (BP11) pulls degree one identically, degree two by
$(a,b)\mapsto a+b$, and degree three to zero. Thus the support
product has two independent degree-two relative classes and one
degree-three class, and the diagonal's exact lost classes are
$\mathbb Q(\alpha_1-\alpha_2)$ and $\mathbb Q(\alpha_1\alpha_2)$.
They are produced by the complete original support product. Their
existence establishes these groups and maps; a Frobenius action or
a positive Weil pairing is not assigned to them without a construction.

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
(P\times P)\blacktriangleright\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z
  \arrow[r,shift left,"\mathrm{pr}_1"]
  \arrow[r,shift right,swap,"\mathrm{pr}_2"]
 &P\blacktriangleright\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z
  \arrow[l,bend left=35,"\mathrm{diag}"]\\
\mathbb Q\alpha_1\oplus\mathbb Q\alpha_2
  \arrow[r,"{(a,b)\mapsto a+b}"]
 &\mathbb Q\alpha
\end{tikzcd}
\caption{The full-support product and its diagonal, (BP9)--(BP13).
The top row displays maps of spectra; the bottom displays the pullback
of relative degree-two cohomology along the diagonal. Both arithmetic
maps are the identity. For the six-point triangle-boundary support,
the product retains all $36$ support points and the additional relative
degree-three class. Sources of the coefficient object and blueprint
operations: version 11 \cite{support} and Lorscheid \cite{lorscheid}.}
\end{figure}

\section{Primitive representatives of the complete arithmetic jet packets}


\subsection{The two endpoint maps and their exact theta splitting}
Keep the original objects
\[
 V=\{\phi\in\mathcal S(\mathbb R):\phi\text{ even},\
       \phi(0)=0,\ \int_{\mathbb R}\phi(x)\,dx=0\},\qquad D=-x\partial_x,
\]
\[
 \begin{gathered}\mathscr B=\{F\in C^\infty(\mathbb R_{>0}):
       \sup_{x>0}|x^bD^kF(x)|<\infty\ (b\in\mathbb Z,\ k\ge0)\},\\
 \quad \Theta\phi(x)=2\sum_{n\ge1}\phi(nx),\quad Q=\mathscr B/\Theta V.
 \end{gathered}\tag{PL1}\label{pl:pl:spaces}
\]
Mellin transforms use \(dx/x\): \(M_+\phi(s)=\int_0^\infty
\phi(x)x^s\,dx/x\) and \(\mathcal M F(s)=\int_0^\infty F(x)x^s\,dx/x\).
The Fourier transform on \(\mathbb R\) has kernel \(e^{-2\pi ix\xi}\).
Poisson summation, with both moments in \(V\) zero, shows that
\(\Theta V\subset\mathscr B\): the sum decays with all Euler derivatives at
infinity, while
\(\Theta\phi(x)=x^{-1}\Theta\widehat\phi(1/x)\) gives the same property
at zero. The two source conventions and their exact scale action are
retained from \cite{FJtau,PLoperator}; Meyer's original treatment
\cite{Meyer}, Theorem \texttt{the:Lap\_range}, provides the human
source context for the full Mellin quotient. Here no closure is added
to the algebraic relation space \(\Theta V\).

Use the actual completion and seed:
\[
 a(s)=s(s-1),\quad
 g(s)=2\xi(s)=a(s)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s),\quad g(0)=g(1)=1,
\]
\[
 \phi_*(x)=(4\pi^2x^4-6\pi x^2)e^{-\pi x^2},\qquad
 A_*(s)=M_+\phi_*(s)=\tfrac12a(s)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2).
 \tag{PL2}\label{pl:pl:seed}
\]
Gaussian integration gives \(\phi_*(0)=0\) and
\(\int_{\mathbb R}\phi_*=4\pi^2(3/(4\pi^2))-6\pi(1/(2\pi))=0\).
The substitution \(t=\pi x^2\) gives the Mellin identity in PL2:
its two terms are \(2\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2+2)\) and
\(-3\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2+1)\). The gamma recurrence gives exactly
the stated factor. No seed coefficient is changed.
For \(\Re s>1\), absolute summation and substitution \(t=nx\) give
\[
 \mathcal M\Theta\phi(s)=2\zeta(s)M_+\phi(s),\qquad
 \mathcal M\Theta\phi_*=g,\qquad \mathcal M DF=s\mathcal M F.
 \tag{PL3}\label{pl:pl:theta-mellin}
\]
The first identity continues across the critical strip and at its
endpoints: \(M_+\phi\) is holomorphic for \(\Re s>-2\), by evenness
and \(\phi(0)=0\), and \(M_+\phi(1)=0\).
Using \(\zeta(0)=-1/2\) and the residue one at \(s=1\) gives
\[
 \ell_0(\phi):=\mathcal M\Theta\phi(0)=-M_+\phi(0),\qquad
 \ell_1(\phi):=\mathcal M\Theta\phi(1)=2M_+'\phi(1).
 \tag{PL4}\label{pl:pl:logmoments}
\]
In particular both endpoint values of \(\Theta\phi_*\) are one.
The operator \(D\) preserves \(V\): it preserves evenness and the
Schwartz conditions, its value at zero is zero, and integration by
parts gives \(\int D\phi=\int\phi=0\). Thus every polynomial in \(D\)
preserves \(V\), and commutes with \(\Theta\).

\begin{theorem}[A primitive representative for every original class]
\label{pl:thm:universal}
Set
\[
 \alpha_0=(1-D)\phi_*,\quad\alpha_1=D\phi_*,
 \quad E_i=\Theta\alpha_i,\quad
 \mathscr B_{\rm pr}=\ker(\mathcal M(\,\cdot\,)(0),\mathcal M(\,\cdot\,)(1)).
 \tag{PL5}
\]
The exact continuous projections
\[
 \mathsf P F=F-\mathcal M F(0)E_0-\mathcal M F(1)E_1,\qquad
 \mathsf P_V\phi=\phi-\ell_0(\phi)\alpha_0-\ell_1(\phi)\alpha_1
 \tag{PL6}\label{pl:pl:projection}
\]
have images \(\mathscr B_{\rm pr}\) and
\[
 V_{\rm pr}=\{\phi\in V:M_+\phi(0)=0,\ M_+'\phi(1)=0\},
 \tag{PL7}
\]
respectively. They satisfy \(\mathsf P\Theta=\Theta\mathsf P_V\) and
give the exact decompositions and quotient isomorphism
\[
 \mathscr B=\mathscr B_{\rm pr}\oplus\C E_0\oplus\C E_1,\qquad
 \Theta V=\Theta V_{\rm pr}\oplus\C E_0\oplus\C E_1,\qquad
 Q\cong\mathscr B_{\rm pr}/\Theta V_{\rm pr}.
 \tag{PL8}\label{pl:pl:quotient}
\]
The last isomorphism is induced by inclusion, with inverse
\([F]\mapsto[\mathsf P F]\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
PL3 gives \(\mathcal M E_0=(1-s)g(s)\) and \(\mathcal M E_1=sg(s)\).
Their endpoint pairs are \((1,0)\) and \((0,1)\), since \(g(0)=g(1)=1\).
This proves idempotence, the asserted images and direct sums.
PL4 gives \(\ell_i(\alpha_j)=\delta_{ij}\), hence the same calculation
on \(V\) proves its projection. Direct substitution proves
\(\mathsf P\Theta=\Theta\mathsf P_V\).
Furthermore \(\Theta V\cap\mathscr B_{\rm pr}=\Theta V_{\rm pr}\), by PL4.
Subtracting the two theta terms in PL6 preserves every original
cohomology class, proving surjectivity and the explicit inverse in
PL8. Its kernel is exactly the stated intersection.
All endpoint functionals are continuous: split their defining
integrals at one and bound by two suitable seminorms from PL1.
All other operators used in PL6 are fixed continuous linear maps.
\end{proof}

The source conditions \(\phi(0)=\int\phi=0\) and the logarithmic
conditions in PL7 have now been joined by actual maps, without
identifying them. Also \(\Theta\) is injective: in \(\Re s>1\) the
Euler product is nonzero, so \(\Theta\phi=0\) forces \(M_+\phi=0\).
Fourier uniqueness in \(\log x\) on any line \(\Re s=c>1\) then
forces \(\phi=0\).

For \(p>1\), write \(U_pF(x)=F(x/p)\), also on \(V\).
It preserves the two primitive spaces, because its endpoint action is
\((b_0,b_1)\mapsto(b_0,pb_1)\). The full projection defect is
\[
 (\mathsf P U_p-U_p\mathsf P)F
 =\Theta\!\left[
 \mathcal M F(0)(U_p\alpha_0-\alpha_0)
 +\mathcal M F(1)(U_p\alpha_1-p\alpha_1)\right].
 \tag{PL9}\label{pl:pl:projection-defect}
\]
Both bracketed differences belong to \(V_{\rm pr}\): their two
endpoint values vanish by the same scaling calculation. Thus the
projection is prime-equivariant on \(Q\), with its precise
representative defect retained.

\subsection{Every multiplicity and every exact packet relation}
Let \(Z\) be a finite nonempty set of actual nontrivial zeros.
Keep every full multiplicity \(m_\rho\), and fix the exact polynomial
\[
 h(s)=c_h\prod_{\rho\in Z}(s-\rho)^{m_\rho},\quad c_h\ne0,\quad
 d=\deg h,\quad U_h(s)=g(s)/h(s),\quad A_h=\C[s]/(h).
 \tag{PL10}\label{pl:pl:packet}
\]
The chosen \(c_h\) is retained throughout. At \(\rho\), \(U_h\) is
a holomorphic unit. Write \(u_h=[U_h]\in A_h^\times\).
For \(u\in A_h\), define the unique polynomial of degree less than \(d\)
\[
 P_u=\rem_h(u_h^{-1}u),\qquad
 R_u=\rem_h([a]^{-1}P_u).
 \tag{PL11}\label{pl:pl:remainders}
\]
The inverse \([a]^{-1}\) exists because no selected zero is \(0\)
or \(1\). Both inverses are full inverses in the nonreduced algebra.

\begin{lemma}[The exact inverse Euler operators]\label{pl:lem:euler}
If \(H\in\mathscr B\) and \(\mathcal M H(\rho)=0\), then
\[
 S_\rho H(x)=x^{-\rho}\int_x^\infty H(t)t^{\rho-1}\,dt
 \tag{PL12}\label{pl:pl:euler}
\]
belongs to \(\mathscr B\), satisfies \((D-\rho)S_\rho H=H\), and has
Mellin transform \(\mathcal M H(s)/(s-\rho)\). Consequently
\[
 S_h=c_h^{-1}\prod_{\rho\in Z}S_\rho^{m_\rho}:
       \ker(j_h\mathcal M)\longrightarrow\mathscr B,\qquad h(D)S_h=1
 \tag{PL13}\label{pl:pl:inverse-h}
\]
is well defined, independent of the order of the factors, and
continuous. Here \(j_h\) denotes all Taylor coefficients modulo \(h\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Differentiation of the tail gives the displayed differential equation.
Put \(x=e^y\), \(\sigma=\Re\rho\), and \(H(e^y)=b(y)\).
For \(y\ge0\), \(|b(y)|\le C_Ne^{-Ny}\) gives
\(|S_\rho H(e^y)|\le C_Ne^{-Ny}/(N-\sigma)\) whenever \(N>\sigma\).
The zero moment changes the same tail at \(y\le0\) into minus the
integral from \(-\infty\) to \(y\). The bound
\(|b(y)|\le C'_Ne^{Ny}\) then gives
\(|S_\rho H(e^y)|\le C'_Ne^{Ny}/(N+\sigma)\) for \(N+\sigma>0\).
The differential equation bounds every derivative in the same way.
These are exactly the seminorm estimates in PL1, and give continuity.
Integration by parts gives the Mellin quotient identity, with its
removable value at \(\rho\).
After one factor is removed, the remaining zero orders of the
transform allow the next factor to be removed; thus PL13 is defined
with the entire multiplicity retained. Each \(D-\rho\) is injective
on \(\mathscr B\), since a homogeneous solution \(C x^{-\rho}\) fails rapid
decrease at at least one end unless \(C=0\). Their product is
injective. Every order of PL13 solves the same equation, so
uniqueness proves order independence.
\end{proof}

Define the original section and its primitive lift by actual elements
of \(\mathscr B\):
\[
 s_hu=S_h\Theta(P_u(D)\phi_*),\qquad
 \operatorname{Prim}_h(u)=a(D)S_h\Theta(R_u(D)\phi_*).
 \tag{PL14}\label{pl:pl:lift}
\]
Their Mellin transforms are \(U_hP_u\) and \(aU_hR_u\).
The domain conditions of PL13 hold because the theta transforms are
\(gP_u\) and \(gR_u\), both divisible by the unaltered \(h\).

\begin{theorem}[Full-jet same-class lift and its exact affine relation]
\label{pl:thm:primitive}
For every \(u\in A_h\),
\[
 j_h\mathcal M s_hu=j_h\mathcal M\operatorname{Prim}_h(u)=u,\qquad
 \operatorname{Prim}_h=\mathsf P s_h,\qquad
 [\operatorname{Prim}_h(u)]=[s_hu]\ \text{in }Q.
 \tag{PL15}\label{pl:pl:sameclass}
\]
More explicitly, the exact division identity and its quotient are
\[
 a(s)R_u(s)-P_u(s)=h(s)T_u(s),\qquad
 T_u(s)=-(1-s)\frac{P_u(0)}{h(0)}-s\frac{P_u(1)}{h(1)},
 \tag{PL16}\label{pl:pl:affine}
\]
and
\[
 \operatorname{Prim}_h(u)-s_hu=\Theta(T_u(D)\phi_*).
 \tag{PL17}\label{pl:pl:exactrelation}
\]
For every polynomial \(P\), the whole polynomial packet family has
the exact relation calculation
\[
 [S_h\Theta(P(D)\phi_*)]=0\text{ in }Q
       \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad h\mid P.
 \tag{PL18}\label{pl:pl:allrelations}
\]
For the primitive family \(a(D)S_h\Theta(P(D)\phi_*)\), the same
condition is necessary and sufficient; if \(P=hT\), its theta
primitive is exactly \(a(D)T(D)\phi_*\in V_{\rm pr}\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The full unit inverse in PL11 proves the first jet identity.
The second follows because \(aR_u-P_u\) is divisible by \(h\).
That difference has degree at most \(d+1\); hence \(T_u\) has degree
at most one. At \(s=0,1\), it has the two values
\(-P_u(0)/h(0)\) and \(-P_u(1)/h(1)\); these uniquely determine
the affine polynomial displayed in PL16, including \(c_h\).
Multiplication of PL16 by \(U_h\) gives \(gT_u\), whose inverse
Mellin transform is \(\Theta(T_u(D)\phi_*)\) by PL3. Mellin
injectivity proves PL17.
The endpoint values of \(s_hu\) are \(P_u(0)/h(0)\) and
\(P_u(1)/h(1)\), since \(g(0)=g(1)=1\).
PL6 and PL16 therefore show that its primitive lift is exactly
\(\mathsf P s_hu\). This proves all of PL15.

If the element in PL18 is \(\Theta\phi\), its full jets at the
selected zeros vanish, by PL3. Its jets are \(u_h[P]\);
the unit \(u_h\) is invertible, so \([P]=0\) in \(A_h\), which is
exactly \(h\mid P\). Conversely, for \(P=hT\), PL13 and injectivity
give \(S_h\Theta(P(D)\phi_*)=\Theta(T(D)\phi_*)\).
Inserting \(a(D)\) proves sufficiency in the primitive case, and
necessity follows because \(a\) is another unit modulo \(h\).
Its primitive source belongs to \(V_{\rm pr}\) because its theta
transform is \(a(s)g(s)T(s)\), with both endpoint values zero.
\end{proof}

In particular the class of \(S_h\Theta\phi_*\) has annihilator
exactly \((h)\) under \(\C[D]\), and its cyclic subspace is
\(\C[s]/(h)\) with its entire nilpotent structure.
Neither PL15 nor PL17 asserts an isometry of a previously chosen
finite source metric or preservation of its polynomial cutoff.
The primitive transform numerator has degree at most \(d+1\),
while \(P_u,R_u\) have degree at most \(d-1\).

\begin{figure}[ht]\centering
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
 A_h \arrow[r,"s_h"] \arrow[dr,"\operatorname{Prim}_h",swap]
       & \mathscr B \arrow[d,"\mathsf P"] \arrow[r,"q"] & Q\\
       & \mathscr B_{\rm pr} \arrow[ur,"q",swap] &
\end{tikzcd}
\caption{The two routes to \(Q\) agree exactly by PL16--17.
Their representative difference is the original theta relation with
the displayed affine polynomial \(T_u\). The full local unit
\(u_h^{-1}\) occurs in both routes through PL11.}
\end{figure}

\subsection{The exact primitive theta relation for every prime action}
Let \(M_s:A_h\to A_h\) be multiplication by \(s\), and
\(\Pi_p=M_{p^s}\), with its full finite Taylor expansion at every zero.
For \(v\in A_h\), define the scalar
\[
 \kappa_h(v)=\frac{sR_v(s)-R_{M_sv}(s)}{h(s)}.
 \tag{PL19}\label{pl:pl:kappa}
\]
This quotient is constant: its numerator is divisible by \(h\)
and has degree at most \(d\). It retains the factor \(c_h^{-1}\)
in polynomial division. Both remainder operations in PL11 commute
with multiplication modulo \(h\), so
\[
 D\operatorname{Prim}_h(v)-\operatorname{Prim}_h(M_sv)
          =\Theta\bigl(\kappa_h(v)a(D)\phi_*\bigr).
 \tag{PL20}\label{pl:pl:D-defect}
\]
This follows by taking Mellin transforms: the difference is
\(aU_h(sR_v-R_{M_sv})=a g\kappa_h(v)\).

\begin{theorem}[The full prime defect is a primitive source relation]
\label{pl:thm:prime}
With \(L_p=\log p\), define the actual Schwartz source
\[
 \psi_{p,u}=
 \int_0^{L_p}\kappa_h(e^{tM_s}u)\,
       U_{e^{L_p-t}}\bigl(a(D)\phi_*\bigr)\,dt.
 \tag{PL21}\label{pl:pl:duhamel}
\]
Then \(\psi_{p,u}\in V_{\rm pr}\) and
\[
 U_p\operatorname{Prim}_h(u)-\operatorname{Prim}_h(\Pi_pu)
                =\Theta\psi_{p,u}.
 \tag{PL22}\label{pl:pl:prime-defect}
\]
Thus the lifted packet action is exactly prime-equivariant in the
primitive quotient of PL8, with a specified relation before taking
that quotient. The defect is zero at all actual full zero jets.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The integrand is a smooth family in \(V\) on a compact interval,
so its integral is in \(V\), with every Schwartz seminorm finite.
The seed \(a(D)\phi_*\) has theta transform \(a g\); its endpoint
values vanish. Every positive dilation preserves those zero endpoint
values, proving that the integral lies in \(V_{\rm pr}\).
Differentiate
\(U_{e^{L_p-t}}\operatorname{Prim}_h(e^{tM_s}u)\)
with respect to \(t\). By PL20 its derivative is
\(-\Theta[\kappa_h(e^{tM_s}u)U_{e^{L_p-t}}a(D)\phi_*]\).
Integration between \(0\) and \(L_p\) proves PL22 with the
displayed sign. The relation space has zero full jets at every
actual zero, by PL3; this proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

Apply every map in PL6--22 at each nonempty original tau support label,
with zero map on the empty label. The transports at degree one are
identities, so all these maps commute with them. Their kernels remain
labelled internal zeros. Thus PL8, PL15 and PL22 also hold in the full
degree-one support diagram and its reconstruction over \(G_L(\C)\).
No numerical coefficient-support observation is used in this passage.

\subsection{The original two-leg complex and its endpoint quotient}
Here is the exact chain calculation, including its degree-zero term.
Write \(\mathcal F\) for the original Fourier transform,
\(\ell=(\ell_0,\ell_1)\), \(b(F)=(\mathcal M F(0),\mathcal M F(1))\),
\(S(b_0,b_1)=(b_1,b_0)\), and
\(A(b_0,b_1)=b_0\alpha_0+b_1\alpha_1\).
The functional equation \(g(1-s)=g(s)\) and Poisson identity give
\(\Theta\mathcal F\phi_* =J\Theta\phi_*\), with Mellin transform
\(g(1-s)=g(s)\). Injectivity of theta gives
\(\mathcal F\phi_*=\phi_*\). Fourier differentiation gives
\(\mathcal F D=(1-D)\mathcal F\); therefore
\[
 \mathcal F A=AS,\quad \ell\mathcal F=S\ell,\quad
 \mathcal F\mathsf P_V=\mathsf P_V\mathcal F,\quad
 \mathcal F V_{\rm pr}=V_{\rm pr}.
 \tag{PL45}\label{pl:pl:Fourier-projection}
\]
The identity for \(\ell\) also follows directly from
\(\mathcal M JF(s)=\mathcal M F(1-s)\). These formulas prove the asserted
commutation by substitution into \(\mathsf P_V=1-A\ell\).

For each original mask \(I\subseteq\{+,-\}\), let
\[
 K_I=[\,V^{I}\xrightarrow{\,d_I\,}\mathscr B\,],\qquad
 d_I(\phi,\psi)=\Theta(\phi-\mathcal F\psi)
 \tag{PL46}
\]
when \(I\ne\varnothing\), with a missing summand interpreted as
the zero coefficient at that retained mask. Let \(K_\varnothing=0\).
Define \(K_{I,\rm pr}\) by replacing the present source copies by
\(V_{\rm pr}\) and the target by \(\mathscr B_{\rm pr}\).
PL45 proves that this is a subcomplex and that the original
projection is the chain map
\((\mathsf P_V^{I},\mathsf P)\).
Its full quotient complex is
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 E_I=[\,(\C^2)^{I}\longrightarrow\C^2\,],\qquad
 (b_+,b_-)\longmapsto b_+-Sb_-,\qquad E_\varnothing=0,\\
 0\longrightarrow K_{I,\rm pr}\longrightarrow K_I
       \xrightarrow{\,(\ell^{I},b)\,}E_I\longrightarrow0 .
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{PL47}\label{pl:pl:endpoint-complex}
\]
The identity \(b\,d_I=\ell\phi-S\ell\psi\) proves that the
displayed surjection is a chain map. Its kernels are exactly
the indicated primitive spaces. A chain section is
\((A^{I},\Theta A)\): its compatibility follows from
\(\mathcal F A=AS\), and its composite with the quotient is the
identity by PL4--6. Thus the sequence splits as complexes.
The quotient, section, and projection all commute with the mask
inclusions, which are coordinate inclusions in degree zero and
identities in degree one.

For a singleton mask the differential of \(E_I\) is either
\(1\) or \(-S\), hence is invertible. For the joint mask its kernel
is \(\{(b,Sb):b\in\C^2\}\), and its differential is onto. Consequently
\[
 H^1(E_I)=0,\qquad
 H^0(E_I)=
 \begin{cases}\C^2,& I=\{+,-\},\\0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}
 \qquad
 H^1(K_{I,\rm pr})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }H^1(K_I).
 \tag{PL48}\label{pl:pl:endpoint-cohomology}
\]
The last map is induced by inclusion. Its inverse is the original
primitive projection on degree-one classes. This proves exactly
which degree-zero directions are removed; it does not identify the
entire two complexes.

Finally retain the source action
\((U_p,pU_{1/p})\) and target action \(U_p\) from the original
tau complex. Put \(D_p=\operatorname{diag}(1,p)\).
On \(E_I\) the respective actions are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 (D_p,SD_pS)\text{ on the present source legs},\qquad
 D_p\text{ on the target},\\
 D_p(b_+-Sb_-)=D_pb_+-S(SD_pS)b_-.
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{PL49}
\]
Thus the short exact sequence PL47 and the isomorphism in PL48
are prime-equivariant. On its joint-mask \(H^0=\C^2\), use
\(b\mapsto(b,Sb)\); the induced action is exactly \(D_p\).
The displayed section is not asserted to be prime-equivariant.
Its target defect is \(U_p\Theta A-\Theta A D_p\), and the two
source defects are \(U_pA-AD_p\) and
\(pU_{1/p}A-ASD_pS\), respectively. Each lands in its primitive
space because its two endpoint values are zero. These are the
chain components of the exact representative discrepancy PL9,
with the Fourier swap and both prime factors retained.

\subsection{The raw pairing and the whole Weil form of this lift}
Assume now that \(Z\) is invariant under
\(\iota\rho=1-\bar\rho\). For the actual local coordinates
\(t_\rho=s-\rho\), retain
\[
 g(\rho+t_\rho)=u_\rho(t_\rho)t_\rho^{m_\rho},
 \qquad u_\rho(0)=2\lambda_\rho,\qquad
 f^\dagger(s)=\overline{f(1-\bar s)}.
 \tag{PL23}
\]
On \(A_h\cong\prod_{\rho\in Z}\mathcal O_\rho/(g)\), use
\[
 R_h(u,v)=\sum_{\rho\in Z}\Res_\rho
       \frac{u^\dagger(s)v(s)}{g(s)}\,ds,\qquad J_g=M_{g'}.
 \tag{PL24}\label{pl:pl:raw}
\]
The germs in this equation may be any representatives.
Divisibility by \(g\) makes a change of representative holomorphic.
The local coefficient matrix of the residue is
\((-1)^j[t^{m_\rho-1-j-k}]u_\rho(t)^{-1}\);
its antidiagonal entries are \((-1)^j/(2\lambda_\rho)\ne0\).
It is therefore perfect, without replacing the unit.
Since \(g'/g=m_\rho/t_\rho+u_\rho'/u_\rho\),
\[
 R_h(u,J_gv)=T_h(u,v)
 =\sum_{\rho\in Z}m_\rho\overline{u(\iota\rho)}v(\rho).
 \tag{PL25}\label{pl:pl:Jacobian}
\]
The full kernel of \(J_g\), and radical of \(T_h\), is
\(\mathfrak n_h=\prod_{\rho\in Z}(t_\rho)\). Indeed locally
\(J_gv=2m_\rho\lambda_\rho v(\rho)t_\rho^{m_\rho-1}\).
The raw pairing detects the complementary higher jets and stays
perfect on those directions.

For \(F,H\in\mathscr B\), define the full reflected zero form
\[
 \mathcal W_g(F,H)=
 \sum_{\rho\in\mathscr Z}m_\rho
    \overline{\mathcal M F(1-\bar\rho)}\mathcal M H(\rho).
 \tag{PL26}\label{pl:pl:Weil}
\]
The sum is absolutely convergent. Repeated integration by parts in
\(\log x\) gives arbitrary inverse powers of \(|\Im\rho|\), uniformly
on \(0\le\Re\rho\le1\). The required zero-count bound also follows
directly from the retained seed. For \(x\ge1\), its Gaussian series
satisfies \(|\Theta\phi_*(x)|\le Cx^4e^{-\pi x^2}\): factor the first
exponential out of each summand and bound the remaining series by
\(\sum_{n\ge1}n^4e^{-\pi(n^2-1)}<\infty\).
The symmetry \(J\Theta\phi_*=\Theta\phi_*\), proved in PL45, gives
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 g(s)=\int_1^\infty\Theta\phi_*(x)(x^s+x^{1-s})\,\frac{dx}{x},
 \\
 \max_{|s|\le R}|g(s)|
 \le C\pi^{-(R+5)/2}\Gamma((R+5)/2)
 \\
 \le \exp(C'(R+1)\log(R+2))\quad(R\ge1).
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{PL50}\label{pl:pl:g-growth}
\]
The gamma bound follows by splitting its integral at one and
bounding its tail by \(n!\), with \(n=\lceil(R+5)/2\rceil\);
\(n!\le n^n\). To count zeros, choose a radius \(r\) between
\(2R\) and \(3R\) whose circle has no zeros. Factor the finitely
many zeros inside that circle out of \(g\). The logarithm of the
modulus of the remaining nonvanishing factor is harmonic.
Its circle average equals its central value; the average of
\(\log|re^{i\theta}-\rho|\) is \(\log r\) for \(|\rho|<r\).
Since \(g(0)=1\), subtraction yields
\(\sum_{|\rho|<r}m_\rho\log(r/|\rho|)
=(2\pi)^{-1}\int_0^{2\pi}\log|g(re^{i\theta})|\,d\theta\).
Each zero with \(|\rho|\le R\) contributes at least \(\log2\).
PL50 therefore bounds their total multiplicity by
\(C''(R+1)\log(R+2)\).
Dyadic summation now proves
\(\sum_\rho m_\rho(1+|\Im\rho|)^{-2N}<\infty\) for \(N\ge2\),
because the zeros lie in the original critical strip.
This proves the absolute convergence asserted in PL26.
The human explicit formula with this transform is recalled below
from \cite{PLConnes}.

\begin{theorem}[Complete packet pullback, with its whole radical]
\label{pl:thm:pairing}
For all full jets \(u,v\in A_h\),
\[
 \mathcal W_g(\operatorname{Prim}_h(u),\operatorname{Prim}_h(v))
 =T_h(u,v)=R_h(u,J_gv).
 \tag{PL27}\label{pl:pl:pullback}
\]
The same full Weil form is obtained using \(s_h\) in either entry.
No zero outside \(Z\) is omitted: its summand is exactly zero.
The primitive family \(\operatorname{Prim}_h(A_h)\) has trace
radical \(\operatorname{Prim}_h(\mathfrak n_h)\), whose elements
are nonzero cohomology classes whenever their jets are nonzero.
The raw residue form transported through the inverse jet map on
this family is perfect.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
At selected zeros, both representative maps have precisely the full
jets \(u,v\), by PL15. At every other actual zero, the transforms
\(U_hP_u\) and \(aU_hR_u\) have the full zero order of \(g\), because
\(h\) does not vanish there. Thus PL26 reduces exactly to PL25.
This proves all equalities with both representatives.
PL15 makes each representative map injective, also after passage to
\(Q\), since theta relations have zero actual jets. The radical is
therefore the exact inverse image of \(\mathfrak n_h\).
Perfectness of the transported raw form follows from PL24.
\end{proof}

A fixed reflected zero contributes \(m_\rho|u(\rho)|^2\).
A nonfixed reflection pair contributes the matrix
\(m_\rho\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\)
on its values. In particular the exact primitive lift transports
both signs of this matrix when such an actual pair is present.
It does not create positivity. The prime identity
\[
 \mathcal W_g(U_pF,U_pH)=p\,\mathcal W_g(F,H)
 \tag{PL28}
\]
follows term by term from
\(\overline{p^{1-\bar\rho}}p^\rho=p\).
PL22 shows that the same identity is induced by \(\Pi_p\) on
the complete primitive packet classes.

\subsection{The same primitive functions for the actual and control zeros}
Keep the supplied exact control constants
\(\delta_0=1/1000,\ \gamma_0=3\). Let \(\gamma_1,\gamma_2\)
be the actual first two positive critical ordinates, and put
\[
 Q_1(s)=((s-\tfrac12)^2+\gamma_1^2)
        ((s-\tfrac12)^2+\gamma_2^2),\qquad U(s)=g(s)/Q_1(s),
\]
\[
 Q_0(s)=((s-\tfrac12)^2-(\delta_0+i\gamma_0)^2)
        ((s-\tfrac12)^2-(\delta_0-i\gamma_0)^2),\qquad
 \widetilde g(s)=U(s)Q_0(s).
 \tag{PL29}\label{pl:pl:control}
\]
The selected actual zeros are simple. The low-height verified theorem
and exhaustive sign-change method in the original author source
\cite{PlattTrudgian}, Theorem \texttt{bank} and Section 2, supply these input facts:
the zero count counts multiplicities, and matching it by distinct
sign changes forces each counted zero to be simple. The same
low-height theorem excludes the off-critical points of \(Q_0\)
from the actual zero set. Thus \(U\) is a unit at every root of
\(Q_0Q_1\). No rounded ordinate is used.

For \(P\in\C[y]_{\le3}\), put \(y(s)=(s-\tfrac12)/i\) and retain
the same entire transform in both forms:
\[
 G_P(s)=a(s)P(y(s))U(s),\qquad F_P=\mathcal M^{-1}G_P.
 \tag{PL30}\label{pl:pl:same-tests}
\]
Its element \(F_P\in\mathscr B_{\rm pr}\) is constructed by PL12--14,
using \(Q_1\) and polynomial \(P(y(s))\); no unproved inverse-transform
membership is required.
Let \(Z_-\) and \(Z_+\) be the roots of \(Q_1,Q_0\), respectively.
On coefficient vectors \(p=(p_0,p_1,p_2,p_3)\), the two evaluation maps are
\[
 (E_\pm p)_z=w_z\sum_{k=0}^3p_k y(z)^k,\qquad
 w_z=a(z)U(z),\quad z\in Z_\pm.
 \tag{PL31}\label{pl:pl:Emaps}
\]
Both maps are invertible: their diagonal factors \(w_z\) are nonzero
and their Vandermonde nodes are distinct.
At \(z=\tau\in Z_-\), the factor is exactly
\(w_\tau=\tau(\tau-1)g'(\tau)/Q_1'(\tau)\).

\begin{theorem}[Actual/control full-Weil contrast and its paired defect]
\label{pl:thm:contrast}
For the same functions \(F_P,F_Q\),
\[
 \mathcal W_g(F_P,F_Q)=p^*E_-^*E_-q,\qquad
 \mathcal W_{\widetilde g}(F_P,F_Q)=p^*E_+^*R_{\rm ref}E_+q,
 \tag{PL32}\label{pl:pl:contrast}
\]
where \(R_{\rm ref}\) exchanges the two members of each of the two
reflected pairs in \(Z_+\). The forms have inertia \((4,0)\) and
\((2,2)\), respectively. These are the entire zero sums.
The exact connecting map and signed defect space are
\[
 C=E_+E_-^{-1}:\C^{Z_-}\longrightarrow\C^{Z_+},\qquad
 \Gamma_C=\{(u,Cu):u\in\C^{Z_-}\},
 \tag{PL33}
\]
\[
 \mathfrak d((u,Cu),(v,Cv))
       =T_{\widetilde g,Z_+}(Cu,Cv)-T_{g,Z_-}(u,v).
 \tag{PL34}\label{pl:pl:paired-defect}
\]
This is the restriction of the two actual Jacobian pairings with
their indicated signs; it is not declared to be an isometry.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
At every actual zero outside \(Z_-\), the transform \(G_P\)
vanishes, so the actual sum is exactly over \(Z_-\).
Those four zeros are critical and simple, giving the first matrix.
For the control, the only remaining nonzero values occur at \(Z_+\):
the retained zeros of \(U\) still annihilate \(G_P\).
The four inserted zeros are simple and form two nonfixed reflected
pairs, giving the second matrix. A Vandermonde matrix is invertible
because a cubic polynomial vanishing at four distinct nodes is zero.
Congruence by each \(E_\pm\) therefore preserves the inertia.
The two reflection blocks have eigenvectors \((1,1),(1,-1)\)
with eigenvalues \(1,-1\), proving the stated counts.
Eliminating the common polynomial coordinates gives exactly \(C\);
subtraction of the two forms gives PL34.
\end{proof}

More explicitly, order one reflected pair as \(\rho_1,\rho_2=\iota\rho_1\)
and put \(\omega_j=y(\rho_j)\) for all four inserted roots.
For the full Lagrange polynomials
\(L_j(y)=\prod_{k\ne j}(y-\omega_k)/(\omega_j-\omega_k)\), take
\[
 P(y)=\frac{L_1(y)}{a(\rho_1)g(\rho_1)/Q_1(\rho_1)}
      -\frac{L_2(y)}{a(\rho_2)g(\rho_2)/Q_1(\rho_2)}.
 \tag{PL35}\label{pl:pl:negative}
\]
Its inserted values are exactly \((1,-1,0,0)\), so
\(\mathcal W_{\widetilde g}(F_P,F_P)=-2\).
It is a nonzero polynomial; the first form in PL32 is positive
definite, so \(\mathcal W_g(F_P,F_P)>0\).
Every actual unit value and phase remains in PL35.

The graph also has an exact prime-action defect. Retain the actual
diagonal actions \((\Pi_{p,-}u)_\tau=p^\tau u_\tau\) and
\((\Pi_{p,+}v)_z=p^zv_z\), and define
\[
 \Delta_{p,n}=C\Pi_{p,-}^{\,n}-\Pi_{p,+}^{\,n}C,\qquad
 \mathfrak D_p(u)=(\Delta_{p,1}u,\ldots,\Delta_{p,4}u)
          \in(\C^{Z_+})^4 .
 \tag{PL51}\label{pl:pl:prime-graph-defect}
\]
For every \(p>1\), this stacked defect map is injective.
To prove it, put \(q_p(X)=\prod_{\tau\in Z_-}(X-p^\tau)\).
Then \(q_p(\Pi_{p,-})=0\). If \(\mathfrak D_p(u)=0\),
the four power identities together with the automatic zeroth
identity give
\(q_p(\Pi_{p,+})Cu=Cq_p(\Pi_{p,-})u=0\).
Every \(p^\tau\) has modulus \(p^{1/2}\), whereas the inserted
eigenvalues have moduli \(p^{1/2+\delta_0}\) or
\(p^{1/2-\delta_0}\). These are different, so
\(q_p(\Pi_{p,+})\) is invertible. Since \(C\) is invertible,
\(u=0\), proving injectivity.
The image of \(\mathfrak D_p\), with its displayed inclusion
in \((\C^{Z_+})^4\), is the exact four-step defect space.
In particular \(\Gamma_C\) contains no nonzero subspace invariant
under the ambient action \((\Pi_{p,-},\Pi_{p,+})\): iteration on
such a subspace would force all four defects to vanish.
This does not erase the connecting map \(C\) or its paired defect.

There is a corresponding exact formula for that pairing.
Set \(\Delta_p=\Delta_{p,1}\), \(T_-=T_{g,Z_-}\), and
\(T_+=T_{\widetilde g,Z_+}\). The individual prime similitudes
on \(T_-\) and \(T_+\) give
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 &\mathfrak d((\Pi_{p,-}u,C\Pi_{p,-}u),
             (\Pi_{p,-}v,C\Pi_{p,-}v))
              -p\,\mathfrak d((u,Cu),(v,Cv))\\
 &\quad=T_+(\Pi_{p,+}Cu,\Delta_pv)
        +T_+(\Delta_pu,\Pi_{p,+}Cv)
        +T_+(\Delta_pu,\Delta_pv).
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{PL52}\label{pl:pl:paired-prime-defect}
\]
Indeed substitute \(C\Pi_{p,-}=\Pi_{p,+}C+\Delta_p\) into
the two slots of PL34 and expand its sesquilinear form.
The two terms without a \(\Delta_p\) scale by \(p\); their
subtraction leaves precisely the three terms displayed.
All phases and unit factors in \(C=E_+E_-^{-1}\) are retained.

For the full-multiplicity extension, take \(Q_-\) to contain the full
actual multiplicities of a reflection-stable packet and let \(Q_+\)
have a disjoint reflection-stable set of nontrivial-strip roots,
none an actual zero. Use equal total degree and the same chosen
leading coefficient in \(Q_+\) and \(Q_-\).
Reflection then multiplies both polynomials by the same factor,
so \((Q_+/Q_-)^\dagger=Q_+/Q_-\) and
\(\widetilde g=gQ_+/Q_-\) retains the reflection involution.
Replace each evaluation matrix by its full Hermite matrix
\(P\mapsto j_{Q_\pm}(aUP)\), where \(U=g/Q_-\).
Multiplication by the two full unit jets and Hermite interpolation
are invertible. The maps in PL33--34 then use the raw residue forms
with denominators \(g=UQ_-\) and \(\widetilde g=UQ_+\), and their
respective Jacobians. The local antidiagonal and contraction
calculation in PL24--25 proves the statement for every jet order;
in particular no higher-jet coefficient is replaced by its value.
The definition of \(\Delta_{p,n}\) and identity PL52 apply unchanged
with these full jet actions. For full packets whose source roots
are critical and target roots are off-critical, the same proof of injectivity
uses \(q_p(X)=\prod_{\tau}(X-p^\tau)^{m_\tau}\) and the first
\(\deg Q_-\) defects. This statement defines and computes the
defect on precisely those full packets; it makes no claim that
all actual roots are critical.

\subsection{Compact primitive representatives and the prime discrepancy}
Define the exact logarithmic change of variables
\[
 (\mathscr LF)(u)=e^{-u/2}F(e^{-u}),\qquad
 \widehat f(z)=\int_{\mathbb R}f(u)e^{-izu}\,du.
 \tag{PL36}
\]
Substitution gives
\(\widehat{\mathscr LF}(z)=\mathcal M F(\tfrac12+iz)\) and
\(\mathscr La(D)=(\partial_u^2-\tfrac14)\mathscr L\).
In particular the two Fourier conditions
\(\widehat f(\pm i/2)=0\) are precisely the two Mellin endpoint
conditions, with their signs kept.

Write \(F_P=a(D)H_P\), where
\(H_P=S_{Q_1}\Theta(P(y(D))\phi_*)\in\mathscr B\), and set
\(h_P=\mathscr LH_P\).
Choose a fixed smooth compactly supported \(\chi\) equal to one
on \([-1,1]\), and put \(\chi_R(u)=\chi(u/R)\). Then
\[
 f_{P,R}=(\partial_u^2-\tfrac14)(\chi_Rh_P)
             \in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R),\qquad
 \widehat f_{P,R}(\pm i/2)=0.
 \tag{PL37}\label{pl:pl:compact}
\]
The last equalities follow by integration by parts against
\(e^{\pm u/2}\), with no boundary term.
Since \(H_P\in\mathscr B\), all derivatives of \(h_P\) decay faster than
every exponential at both ends. Thus \(f_{P,R}\to\mathscr LF_P\)
in every exponentially weighted smooth seminorm.
In particular, for any integer \(N\), integration by parts gives
a sequence \(\epsilon_R\to0\) such that, uniformly for
\(0\le\sigma\le1\),
\[
 |\widehat f_{P,R}((\sigma-\tfrac12)/i+t)
          -G_P(\sigma+it)|\le\epsilon_R(1+|t|)^{-N}.
 \tag{PL38}\label{pl:pl:approx}
\]
Here the Fourier argument is \(t+i(1/2-\sigma)\); hence
\(1/2+i[t+i(1/2-\sigma)]=\sigma+it\), fixing its sign.

For either zero set, let \(C_N^2=\sum_\rho
m_\rho(1+|\Im\rho|)^{-2N}\), finite for \(N\ge2\), and use its
weighted value norm \(\|v\|^2=\sum m_\rho|v_\rho|^2\).
PL38 bounds the norm difference by \(C_N\epsilon_R\).
The reflection permutation has norm one, so
\[
 |\mathcal W(F_{P,R},F_{P,R})-\mathcal W(F_P,F_P)|
 \le C_N\epsilon_R(2\|v(F_P)\|+C_N\epsilon_R).
 \tag{PL39}\label{pl:pl:error}
\]
This is the entire zero sum, including every zero outside the
selected packets. Apply it to both the actual and control zero sets.
For the nonzero polynomial PL35, the actual value is positive
and the control value is \(-2\). Taking the right side of PL39
less than half the positive actual value and less than one,
respectively, proves that both opposite signs persist for all
sufficiently large \(R\) within the compact primitive class.
These approximants need not retain the original theta class;
PL15 gives the exact same-class lift, while PL37 gives the
separate compact approximation with its proved topology and error.

For completeness, the rational-prime arithmetic distinguishes the
two forms exactly. Set
\[
 H(s)=\overline{G_P(1-\bar s)}G_Q(s),\qquad
 k(x)=\frac1{2\pi i}\int_{\Re s=c}H(s)x^{-s}\,ds\quad(c>1).
 \tag{PL40}\label{pl:pl:correlation}
\]
Products of Mellin transforms from \(\mathscr B\) are transforms of their
multiplicative convolutions, so \(k\in\mathscr B\) and \(\mathcal M k=H\).
The classical explicit formula of Guinand and Weil, in the
convention written in the original author source \cite{PLConnes},
equations \texttt{bombieriexplicit}--\texttt{bombieriexplicit2}, is
\[
 \mathcal W_g(F_P,F_Q)
 =H(0)+H(1)
 -\sum_p(\log p)\sum_{r\ge1}\bigl(k(p^r)+p^{-r}k(p^{-r})\bigr)
 -\mathcal W_\infty(k),
 \tag{PL41}\label{pl:pl:explicit}
\]
\[
 \mathcal W_\infty(k)=(\log(4\pi)+\gamma_E)k(1)
 +\int_1^\infty
 \left(k(x)+x^{-1}k(x^{-1})-\frac2x k(1)\right)
                    \frac{dx}{x-x^{-1}}.
 \tag{PL42}
\]
The numerator vanishes at \(x=1\); all infinite-endpoint terms
converge by \(k\in\mathscr B\). Both pole terms in PL41 vanish for these
primitive tests. This invocation is a cited human explicit-formula
theorem, not a newly assumed positivity assertion.

The control has the additional exact rational term
\[
 d(s)=Q_0'(s)/Q_0(s)-Q_1'(s)/Q_1(s),
 \qquad
 \mathcal W_{\widetilde g}(F_P,F_Q)-\mathcal W_g(F_P,F_Q)
       =\sum_{z\in Z_+}H(z)-\sum_{\tau\in Z_-}H(\tau).
 \tag{PL43}\label{pl:pl:rational}
\]
The second equality follows directly by subtracting the two full
zero multisets; every retained zero and multiplicity cancels.
It is also exactly
\[
 \frac1{2\pi i}\int_{\Re s=c}
                    d(s)\bigl(H(s)+H(1-s)\bigr)\,ds.
 \tag{PL44}
\]
Indeed \(d(1-s)=-d(s)\). Integrate \(dH\) around a rectangle with
vertical sides \(c\) and \(1-c\), both outside the finite roots.
The horizontal integrals tend to zero because \(d\) is rational
and \(H\) decays rapidly in this fixed strip. Substitution
\(s\mapsto1-s\) in the left integral produces PL44. The residues
are \(+H(z)\) at each inserted root and \(-H(\tau)\) at each
removed root, proving equality with PL43.
Thus using the actual prime expression PL41 for the control
without PL43 would change the arithmetic form. The paired defect
PL34 records precisely this difference on the common primitive
family. No universal positivity or RH conclusion follows.


\section{The complete signed support and the absolute twistor charts}


\subsection{Human source and the exact categories}

The source is Alain Connes and Caterina Consani,
\emph{The Absolute Twistor Line and the Geometry of
$\overline{\Spec\Z}$},
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00299v1}{arXiv:2609.00299v1}.
The original author file \path{CC.tex} was read at lines
310--413 and 420--1207, including the entire relevant sections on
the signed base, the Lydakis--Day product and proposed coproduct,
the two charts, the geometric involution, complex conjugation,
and odd Frobenius. Exact source locators are
\texttt{F2}, \texttt{sect2}, \texttt{sect2.2}, \texttt{conv},
\texttt{opens}, \texttt{eq:restriction\_maps},
\texttt{def:alpha\_symmetry}, \texttt{thm:twistor\_space\_scheme},
and \texttt{prop:twistor\_frobenius}.

The calculations below first take place in commutative pointed
monoids. Their zero is absorbing and their identity is denoted $1$.
Put
\[
 B=\{0,1,\epsilon\},\qquad\epsilon^2=1,\quad\epsilon\ne1.
 \tag{AT1}\label{at:eq:signed-base}
\]
A signed pointed monoid is supplied with a morphism from $B$.
The target may specialize the sign, but all universal calculations
retain $\epsilon\ne1$. The source identifies spherical monoids'
tensor product with the relative smash product. We therefore write
$\otimes_B$ for that pointed-monoid pushout: in particular
$\epsilon\otimes1=1\otimes\epsilon$. No additive relation is
introduced until an explicitly named ring or semiring receiver.

\subsection{The actual full split base and its two spectra}

Let $L$ be a finite nontrivial bounded distributive lattice. Retain
\[
 G_L(\Z)=\{z_\lambda=(0,\lambda):\lambda\in L\}
       \cup\{\widehat a=(a,1_L):a\in\Z\}.
 \tag{AT2}\label{at:eq:full-semiring}
\]
Its overlap is $e=z_{1_L}=\widehat0$, while
$\tau=z_{0_L}\ne e$. Addition is coefficient addition and lattice
join; multiplication is coefficient multiplication and lattice
meet. Thus $\widehat a z_\lambda=z_\lambda$ and
$z_\lambda z_\mu=z_{\lambda\wedge\mu}$. Let $M_L$ be its
underlying multiplicative pointed monoid with zero $\tau$.
There is an injective signed-base map
\[
 B\longrightarrow M_L,
 \qquad 0\mapsto\tau,\quad1\mapsto\widehat1,
 \quad\epsilon\mapsto\widehat{-1}.
 \tag{AT3}\label{at:eq:base-map}
\]
In particular $\epsilon z_\lambda=z_\lambda$ for every support
label. This relation is retained below, including for $e$.

For precision a prime of a pointed monoid is a proper ideal whose
complement is multiplicatively closed; additive closure is not
part of this definition. The entire pointed-monoid spectrum of
$M_L$ can be computed:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 P_c&=\{z_\lambda:c\not\le\lambda\},&&0_L<c\in L,\\
 Q_E&=\{z_\lambda:\lambda\in L\}
  \cup\{\widehat a:a\ne0,\ p\mid a\text{ for some }p\in E\},
  &&E\subset\{\text{rational primes}\}.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{AT4}\label{at:eq:monoid-primes}
\]
To prove exhaustion, an ideal containing any supported coefficient
contains $e$ by multiplication by $e$, and hence every support
label. Its remaining prime condition is exactly that on the free
commutative monoid of positive prime factors, with signs units;
unique integer factorization gives $Q_E$. An ideal omitting $e$
contains no supported coefficient. Its support labels form a
downset whose complement is a nonempty meet-closed up-set. Because
$L$ is finite, that complement is $\mathord\uparrow c$ for the
meet $c$ of its elements, and $c\ne0_L$. This gives $P_c$.
Both displayed families directly satisfy the ideal and prime
conditions, proving the classification.

The original semiring spectrum embeds topologically in this monoid
spectrum by retaining exactly the additively closed primes. In
the support family these are the $P_c$ with $c$ join prime, or
equivalently join irreducible in a finite distributive lattice.
Indeed join closure of its complement's complement is exactly
$c\le\lambda\vee\mu\Rightarrow c\le\lambda$ or $c\le\mu$.
In the arithmetic family they are $Q_\varnothing$ and
$Q_{\{p\}}$: two different primes in $E$ generate $1$ additively
by their integer Bezout identity, and a singleton gives the usual
prime ideal. Its subspace topology is the original one, since
